契切林（Chicherin）那位脾气暴躁、举止粗鄙生硬的机要秘书罗森堡（Rosenberg）位高权重却极不得人心，他耀武扬威地通知我，自己奉命即将启程奔赴远东“执行一项十万火急的机密要务”。顺带地，仿佛是突然想起了什么微不足道的闲杂琐事一般，他漫不经心地随口提到了“明天的葬礼”；听到这个惊天噩耗我如遭五雷轰顶：约翰·里德（John Reed）不幸英年早逝了！考察队原定今晚启程赶赴彼得格勒，但我们毅然决然决定推迟行程，以便向这位长眠的挚友战友致以最后的崇高敬意。
克里姆林宫红墙脚下、正对着红场的一侧，新掘开了一座墓穴，这里是安葬革命殉难英烈长眠的无上光荣圣地。我伫立在墓穴边缘，搀扶着身心彻底崩溃、痛不欲生的路易丝·布赖恩特（Louise Bryant）。在历经漫长的两地分离之后，她历经千难万险从美洲一路风尘仆仆飞奔而来同杰克（约翰·里德）重聚。在彼得格勒扑空之后，她一路辗转赶赴莫斯科，却赫然获悉里德已奉密令开拔赶往巴库（Baku）参加东方各民族代表大会。当时他刚从芬兰暗无天日的铁窗酷刑中侥幸脱身，身体元气尚未平复，内心极不情愿在严寒中踏上如此凶险劳顿的长途跋涉。然而季诺维也夫（Zinoviev）态度蛮横坚决；季诺维也夫声称大会必须有美国无产阶级的正式代表出席，而杰克像一名服从纪律的忠诚党的好战士一样，默默服从了调遣。然而他那虚弱衰竭的体魄，根本无法抵御苏俄境内极端恶劣的严寒颠簸以及四处肆虐的致命斑疹伤寒感染。里德被紧急护送抬回莫斯科时已病入膏肓。尽管全俄最顶尖的医学专家倾尽全力抢救，他终究未能战胜死神，于10月16日溘然长逝。
苍穹被一片铅灰色的浓云密布笼罩。天空中飘洒着冰冷刺骨的凄风苦雨与漫天雨夹雪。在发言者一段接一段的悼词间隙，冰冷的雨滴无情敲击在杰克的棺木上，发出一阵阵沉闷刺耳的声响，宛如一颗颗冰冷的铁钉正在狠狠凿入这具薄板棺木。官方发表的悼文言辞考究、字正腔圆，如同一颗颗圆润冰冷的雨滴无情砸在听众耳畔，显得空洞冰冷毫无意义。路易丝瘫软蜷缩在泥泞湿漉的冰冷泥地上。我竭尽全力将她从地上搀扶起来，几乎是硬生生架着她站直身躯。她眼神呆滞空洞陷入极度精神恍惚，对身旁共产党吊唁者们高谈阔论的悼词全然充耳不闻。布哈林、赖因斯坦以及欧洲和美洲各共产党支部的代表们，慷慨激昂地大肆颂扬着世界革命的英勇先锋队，而路易丝则痛不欲生、十指死死抠住那具粗糙的薄木棺木。唯有曾与杰克朝夕相处、同甘共苦结下深厚革命情谊的年轻同袍费奥多索夫，用发自肺腑的真挚追忆在这刺骨冰冷的雨雪中洒下了一缕人性的温存阳光。柯伦泰深情追忆了杰克生前那光明磊落、豪爽纯真的崇高人格与赤子之心。她怀着锥心刺骨的真诚严厉扪心自问——约翰·里德的英年早逝，难道不正是由于我们对真正同志情谊的冷漠疏忽所酿成的无法挽回的惨剧吗？！……

===Original===

---
The Museum is highly pleased with the success of our Expedition. In token of appreciation the Board of Directors requested us, to continue our work in the Crimea, now entirely freed from White forces. The mandate for the car has been prolonged till the close of the year.
But the independent, nonpartisan character of the Museum activities is apparently displeasing to Communist circles in Moscow. They contend that the capital, rather than Petrograd, should be the home of such an institution. The idea is sponsored, it is said, in opposition to the growing power of Zinoviev. Certain influences are at work to curtail the Museum’s sphere of action. With surprise we learn that a special body has been created in the center with exclusive authority to collect material concerning the history of the Russian Communist Party. The new organization, known as the Ispart, by virtue or its Communist character, claims control over the Museum and has announced its intention of directing future expeditions.
The situation threatens the efficiency of our work. By request of the Museum, I have repeatedly visited Moscow in an endeavor to reach an amicable understanding. Lunacharsky, with whom I have discussed the matter, admits the justice of our position. But the Ispart continues to assert its supremacy, claims the right to our car, and insists on controlling the expeditions by placing a political commissar in charge.
The attitude of the Ispart is inimical to free initiative and best effort. It is also indicative of a lack of confidence. If persisted in, it would exclude my further cooperation. Under no conditions could I consent to the supervision of a commissar, whose duties are virtually identical with spying and denunciation. Several of my colleagues in the Expedition, including Emma Goldman, share this viewpoint.
During the negotiations it has been suggested that we visit the Far North to gather the historic data of the period of Allied occupation and the Provisional Government of Tchaikovsky. The Ispart professes no interest in the undertaking and foregoes control over it. We welcome the opportunity and decide to make a short journey to Archangel via Moscow, where formalities are to be completed.
In the capital we find our friends in great consternation. The Bolsheviki, it is charged, have treacherously broken their agreement with Makhno.

===Chinese===

---
博物馆董事会对我们考察队的辉煌考察成果给予了高度赞誉。为表嘉奖与信任，董事会正式恳请我们乘胜追击，继续前往刚刚从白卫军铁蹄下彻底解放的克里米亚半岛展开文献搜集。我们专列车厢的配属特许使用权也被破例顺延批准延长到了今年年底。
然而，博物馆独立自主、秉持非党派立场的科学工作作风，显然在莫斯科的共产党官僚核心圈子里激起了极大的不满与猜忌。他们蛮横宣称，作为国家首都的莫斯科，理应比彼得格勒更有资格设立并掌控此类国家级史料机构。坊间私下盛传，这项争权夺利的动议纯粹是某些官僚为了敲打遏制季诺维也夫日益膨胀的权势而处心积虑炮制的。一股股不可告人的幕后暗流正在紧锣密鼓地策划扼杀博物馆的自主权限。令我们惊愕不已的是，中央竟然破天荒突击成立了一个专门机构——俄共（布）党史委员会（Ispart），被中央独家授予统揽搜集整理全俄共产党历史档案文献的至高无上全权。这个新出炉的特权机构凭借其纯粹共产党的党派血统背景，不可一世地宣称自己对革命博物馆享有全面领导管辖权，并高调宣布将全权统领指挥未来的每一次考察活动。
这一争权夺利的霸道行径对我们考察工作的科学效率构成了致命威胁。受博物馆全权委托，我曾数度风尘仆仆往返奔赴莫斯科，竭尽全力试图寻求和平友好的妥协解决方案。我曾与主管教育与文化的卢那察尔斯基（Lunacharsky）当面深谈此案，卢那察尔斯基在私下坦承我们的立场完全合情合理。然而党史委员会却飞扬跋扈不依不饶，执意宣示其至高无上的绝对领导权，强硬索要剥夺我们专列车厢的支配权，并蛮横坚持必须向考察队强行指派一名全权政治政委以实施思想与政治监控。
党史委员会的跋扈姿态不仅是对民间自由探索与科学求实精神的公然扼杀，更赤裸裸暴露了体制对知识界的不信任与敌意防范。倘若当局一意孤行执迷不悟，我将彻底退出考察队断绝与其的一切合作。在任何情况下，我都绝不可能接受一名政治政委的监视骑跨——因为这项职务的本质，与搜集黑材料告密刺探的克格勃式特务密探毫无二致。考察队中的多名同仁，包括爱玛·戈尔德曼在内，在此问题上与我立场高度一致休戚与共。
在旷日持久的拉锯谈判期间，有人提议考察队何不转道北上远赴远北方地区，搜集关于英法协约国武装干涉占领时期以及柴可夫斯基北方临时政府时期的第一手珍贵历史文献。党史委员会对此项课题毫无兴趣，并主动放弃了对该路线的政治插手与控制权。我们对此项转机深表欢迎，当即拍板决定经由莫斯科办理相关出差手续后，对阿尔汉格尔斯克展开一次短途科学考察。
一抵达首都莫斯科，我们便发现城里的挚友同志们正陷入极度的惊骇与义愤之中。大家群情激愤地怒斥布尔什维克当局卑鄙背信弃义，单方面撕毁了同马赫诺签署的同盟停火协议。

===Original===

No sooner had the *povstantsi* helped to defeat Wrangel than Trotsky ordered their disarmament. They were surrounded and attacked by Red forces, but succeeded in extricating themselves, and now open warfare has again been declared. Meanwhile the Anarchists, unaware of these developments, had gathered from all parts of the country in Kharkov, where a conference was to be held December Ist, in accordance with Makhno’s agreement with the Bolsheviki. All of them, together with many local Anarchists, were arrested, among them my friends Volin and Baron, widely known as men of high idealism and revolutionary devotion. The greatest fear is felt for their safety.

===Chinese===

起义游击队（povstantsi）在前线刚刚协助主力彻底歼灭弗兰格尔（Wrangel）大军，托洛茨基（Trotsky）便立刻翻脸不认人，向游击队下达了全员缴械的最后通牒。游击队大队人马随即遭到红军主力部队的重重包围与闪电突袭，但游击战士们凭借勇猛拼死杀出了一条血路突围而去，如今双方再度爆发了公开的全面武装冲突。与此同时，蒙在鼓里对此惊天剧变毫不知情的全俄无政府主义代表们，此前正按照马赫诺同布尔什维克达成的同盟协议条款，从全国各个角落风尘仆仆赶赴哈尔科夫（Kharkov），准备出席原定于12月1日举行的全俄代表大会。结果，这批代表连同当地大批无政府主义知名学者遭到了一网打尽的集体逮捕，其中就包括我的至交挚友沃林（Volin）与巴隆（Baron）——他们素以崇高的道德节操与无私的献身精神名扬全俄。如今，所有人都在为他们的生死安危捏一把冷汗。

## XXXVI In the Far North / XXXVI 在极北之地
===Original===

*December, 1920.*﻿—Yaroslavl, an ancient city, is picturesque on the banks of the Volga. Very impressive are its cathedrals and monasteries, fine specimens of the architectural art of northeastern Russia of feudal times. But desolate is the sight of the many demolished buildings and churches. On the opposite side of the river the whole district is wrecked by artillery and fire. Dismal reminders of the harrowing days of June, 1918, when the counterrevolutionary insurrection led by Savinkov, once famous terrorist, was crushed. More than a third of the city was destroyed, its population reduced by half.
The shadow of that tragedy broods darkly over Yaroslavl. The hand laid upon the rebels was so heavy, its imprint is still felt. The people are cowed, terrified at the very mention of the ghastly days of June.
Through Vologda we reach Archangel, at the mouth of the Northern Dvina, almost within the Arctic Circle. The city is situated on the right bank, separated from the railroad station by the river which we cross on foot. The ice is dotted with peasant sleighs, some drawn by reindeer with huge, crooked antlers. The drivers are entirely wrapped in furs, only their narrow dark eyes and flat Lapland noses visible.
The streets are clean, the small frame houses well kept. “We have learned from the Occupation,” Kulakov, Chairman of the Ispelkom, comments. He is a young man, tall, clean featured, and of quick intelligence. The Whites killed his entire family, including his young sister, but Kulakov has preserved his mental balance and humanity.
Comparative order prevails in the Soviet institutions. The long queues so characteristic of Bolshevik managements are almost entirely absent. The natives have acquired method and efficiency; “from the example of the Americans,” they frankly admit. There is scarcity of provisions, but the *pyock* is more equitable, distribution more systematic than elsewhere. The Communists are the dominant factor, but they are encouraging the cooperation of the other elements of the population. Experience has taught them to economize human life. Many former White officers are employed, even in responsible positions. Their services are very satisfactory, I am told; particularly in the schools they are of help. Even monks and nuns have been given opportunity to serve the people.

===Chinese===

1920年12月。——雅罗斯拉夫尔是一座历史悠久的千年古城，依傍着美丽的伏尔加河畔，风景如画。城内的大教堂与修道院令人叹为观止，堪称封建割据时期俄罗斯东北部建筑艺术的绝美典范。然而，举目所见大批惨遭拆毁坍塌的民房与教堂，却呈现出一片令人心碎的凄凉破败。在河流对岸，整个街区几乎全被当年的炮火与大火夷为平地。这些都是1918年6月那场惨绝人寰血腥风暴的刺目遗迹——当时由昔日大名鼎鼎的恐怖分子萨文科夫（Savinkov）亲自领导的反革命叛乱最终遭到残酷镇压。全城超过三分之一的建筑被彻底摧毁，城市人口锐减了整整一半。
那场悲剧的浓重阴影，至今如幽灵般黑压压笼罩在雅罗斯拉夫尔上空。当年强加在叛乱者头上的镇压铁拳是何等酷烈，其深深的烙印至今依然清晰可感。全城百姓被彻底吓破了胆，一提起当年6月那段人间地狱般的恐怖岁月便心惊胆战面无人色。
取道沃洛格达，我们终于抵达了北德维纳河入海口处、几乎已切入北极圈内的阿尔汉格尔斯克。市区坐落在河流右岸，与火车站隔河相望，我们只能徒步踏冰穿过冰封的江面。冰面上零星点缀着农夫拉货的雪橇，有的雪橇套着头顶巨大弯曲鹿角的驯鹿。赶车人全身严严实实裹在厚重的毛皮大衣里，只露出一双狭长黝黑的眼眸与拉普兰人特有的扁平鼻梁。
大街小巷干净整洁，一栋栋精巧的木屋维护得井井有条。“我们这是从外国占领军身上吸取了经验教训，”执行委员会主席库拉科夫（Kulakov）意味深长地评论道。他是一位身材挺拔、五官清秀、头脑极其敏锐精明的年轻才俊。白卫军当年杀害了他全家老小，连他年幼的亲妹妹也未能幸免，但库拉科夫却奇迹般保全了内心的理智平和与博大人性。
与其他苏维埃城市相比，当地各苏维埃机关衙门里展现出了难能可贵的相对井然秩序。布尔什维克官僚体制下那种司空见惯、令人绝望的冗长排队长龙在这里几乎完全绝迹。本地干部行事极具条理章法与办事效率；“这完全是照搬学样美利坚人的作风，”他们毫不避讳地坦承道。粮食供应固然吃紧匮乏，但这里的学术口粮（pyock）分配要公允得多，发放体系也远比其他地方更为系统周密。共产党人虽是掌控大局的支配力量，却在主动热情鼓励联合全城其他社会各阶层携手共事。血淋淋的惨痛现实教会了他们必须百般珍惜爱护人力资源。大批旧沙皇时期的白军军官被重新起用，甚至官居要职。据反映他们的履职表现极其出色令人满意；尤其是在中小学教育岗位上，这些军官发挥了不可或缺的巨大作用。甚至连修道院里的僧侣与修女，也破例获得了为人民效劳出力的广阔舞台。

===Original===

Some art workshops are managed by former inmates of monasteries, still in their religious garb, sewing and embroidering for the children and instructing them in the art.
The orphan homes and asylums we have visited, unannounced, are clean and tidy, the inmates warmly dressed and of healthy appearance, their relations with the teachers very harmonious, even affectionate.
Speculation in food has entirely ceased. The old marketplace is almost deserted; only small articles of apparel are offered for sale. The distributing Soviet centers still have some of the provisions, mostly canned goods, left by the American Mission, which is remembered with respect, almost with regret. But considerable sentiment is felt against the English, who are charged with political partisanship in the civil struggle of the North. The story is told of the destruction of huge supplies, sunk in the Dvina in the very sight of the starved population, by order of General Rollins, who was in charge of the British evacuation.
The cordial cooperation of Kulakov and other Communist officials has enabled us to collect valuable material on the history of the Provisional Government of the Northern District. A pitiful picture it presents of Tchaikovsky, once the “grandfather of the Russian Revolution.” Foreswearing his glorious past, he served as the vassal of Kolchak, whom he servilely acknowledged as the “Supreme Ruler of Russia.” So impotent, however, was the role of Tchaikovsky that his regime is contemptuously referred to as the “Government of Miller,” the commander-in-chief of the counterrevolutionary forces which escaped to Norway soon after the British evacuation.
Though comprising but a small part of the population, the workers of Archangel (about 3,000 out of a total of 50,000) have played a decisive part in the history of the city and district. In the union gatherings I have come in touch with intelligent proletarian groups whose independence and self-reliance is the key to the local situation. Far from “the center” and small in number, the Bolsheviki are vitally dependent on the labor element in the management of affairs. Party dictatorship has been mitigated by the actual participation of the toilers. Their influence is restraining and salutary.
Betchin, the Chairman of the Union Soviet, personifies the history and spirit of the whole revolutionary epoch.

===Chinese===

某些工艺美术作坊由修道院的还俗修女出面打理掌管，她们依然身披传统的宗教修士袍服，专心致志地为孤儿贫童缝制刺绣衣物，并向孩子们传授美术刺绣手艺。
我们未打招呼突击走访视察的孤儿院与收容所，处处干净整洁窗明几净，收容的孩子们衣着暖和厚实，面色红润健康，师生之间的感情极其融洽和睦，甚至亲如一家。
食品黑市投机倒把活动已在此地彻底绝迹。古老的集市广场冷落荒凉几乎空无一人；唯有零星变卖旧衣服的摊位在惨淡经营。苏维埃官方配给中心手里至今还储备着相当数量的食品物资，主要是当年美洲代表团撤离时遗留下的整箱罐头食品，本地民众对美洲代表团念念不忘怀着由衷的敬重与眷恋。然而，大众对英国人却怀有刻骨的敌意反感，民间群情激愤指责英国人在北方的残酷内战中大搞政治偏袒拉偏架。坊间至今愤慨流传着这样一桩公案：当年主持英军撤退疏散的罗林斯将军（General Rollins）竟然丧心病狂地下令，当着全城嗷嗷待哺、饥肠辘辘的饥民的面，将堆积如山的军用给养物资统统沉入北德维纳河底活活销毁。
在库拉科夫以及其他共产党领导干部的真诚鼎力协作下，我们搜集到了大批详实记录北方地区临时政府历史演进的珍贵文献档案。这些材料勾勒出了一幅关于柴可夫斯基（Tchaikovsky）——这位昔日曾被誉为“俄国革命的祖父”的元老泰斗——何其可悲屈辱的凄凉画卷。他悍然背叛了自己早年光辉灿烂的革命履历，甘愿沦为高尔察克（Kolchak）膝下的儿皇帝家奴，奴颜婢膝地公开俯首尊奉高尔察克为“全俄最高统治者”。然而，柴可夫斯基在傀儡政权中扮演的角色又是何等软弱窝囊无能，以至于他的伪政权在坊间被极尽轻蔑地直接讥讽为“米勒政府”（Government of Miller）——而米勒正是那位在英军前脚刚撤退疏散之后、后脚便仓皇出逃流亡挪威的反革命武装总司令。
阿尔汉格尔斯克的产业工人尽管在全城人口中仅占微不足道的一小部分（在全城50000总人口中仅约有3000名产业工人），却在这座城市乃至整个北疆的历史风云中扮演了举足轻重的决定性角色。在各工会集会上，我结识了一批批极具政治觉悟的产业工人知识骨干，他们独立自主、自力更生的傲骨气魄正是解开本地政治谜题的核心金钥匙。由于天高皇帝远远离“中央”，且党员人数微乎其微，当地布尔什维克在管理社会公共事务时不得不极端依赖倚重这批产业工运中坚。劳动群众实实在在的全面参与，极大地稀释遏制了教条主义的一党专制。工人们的影响是清醒、克制且大有裨益的。
工会苏维埃主席别钦（Betchin）本人，堪称整整一部波澜壮阔革命史诗与时代崇高精神的具象化化身。

===Original===

Tall and stockily built, of plain speech and convincing honesty, he is typical of the Northern worker. In his person the Provisional Government had sought to suppress the rebellious element of the Archangel proletariat. Popular labor man and member of the local Duma, Betchin was indicted for treason. The central figure of the celebrated trial known by his name, he was condemned to a living death in the terrible prison at Iokange in the frozen North. But his conviction served to consolidate the wavering ranks against the Provisional Government; his name became the slogan of the united opposition. Its rising waves drove the Allies from Archangel and Murmansk, and abolished Tchaikovsky’s regime.
With much difficulty I persuaded the modest Betchin to donate his picture and autobiographic sketch to the “revolutionary gallery” of the Museum. His friends informed me that upon his return to prison he insisted that his portraits be removed from the union headquarters. In appreciation of our mission he presented us with the old crimson banner of the Soviet, battle-scarred in numerous campaigns.
“We are working in harmony with the several factions in the unions,” Betchin said. “The welfare of the people is our sole aim, and on that platform we can all agree, whatever our political predilections.”
With a smile of indulgent reminiscence he admitted his former adherence to the Social-Democrats. “But we have no more Mensheviki here,” he hastened to add; “we all joined the Bolsheviki long ago.”
“The comrade from the center probably doesn’t know how it happened,” his assistant remarked. “Revolutionary life sometimes plays curious pranks. You see,” he continued, “word had reached us that the Mensheviki and S.R.’s in Moscow had gone over to the Bolsheviki. We decided to do the same. That’s how we happen to be Communists now. But the report later proved false,” he concluded with a touch of disappointment.
“We have never regretted it,” Betchin said soberly.
There is no direct railroad line between Archangel and Murmansk, and we are compelled to make the long journey back to Vologda in order to reach the Coast. In Petrosavodsk we learn that owing to the unusually severe snowstorms the trip cannot be undertaken at present. It is Christmas Eve; before the end of the year we are pledged to return to Petrograd. To our great regret the journey further north must be abandoned.

===Chinese===

身材高大魁梧、说话朴实无华、处事光明磊落令人信服，他是北方工人阶级最杰出的典型代表。在他身上，昔日的北方临时政府曾千方百计企图掐灭扼杀阿尔汉格尔斯克无产阶级的反抗叛逆火种。作为深得民心的工人领袖兼地方杜马议员，别钦当年曾被伪政权以叛国大罪正式起诉。作为轰动全俄的那场以其姓名命名的著名公审的核心主角，他被判处流放投入极北冰原上阴森恐怖的约坎加（Iokange）死牢，经受人间地狱般的折磨摧残。然而对他的政治迫害，反而空前团结凝聚了广大群众动摇犹豫的阵营起而反抗临时政府；他的光辉大名成为了全城统一反对派的核心战斗号角。这股汹涌澎湃的反抗巨浪最终将英美协约国干涉军彻底赶出了阿尔汉格尔斯克与摩尔曼斯克，将柴可夫斯基傀儡政权彻底扫进了历史的垃圾堆。
在我的再三软磨硬泡苦苦劝说下，为人极度谦逊低调的别钦终于勉强同意将他的一张个人照片与亲笔自传手稿捐赠给博物馆的“革命英雄殿堂”。他的同志私下悄悄向我透露，当年他从地牢苦役凯旋归来之后，曾严厉下令勒令工会总部必须立刻摘下所有悬挂的领袖个人肖像。为表达对我们考察使命的崇高敬意，他郑重将一面在无数次枪林弹雨血战中弹痕累累的工会苏维埃深红色革命老战旗赠予我们珍藏。
“我们一直同工会内部的各个政治派系风雨同舟精诚合作，”别钦语重心长地说道。“全心全意为劳苦大众谋福祉是我们唯一的奋斗纲领；在这个神圣的平台上，无论各自抱有何种政治倾向政见，我们大家都能达成高度共识。”
带着一丝宽容包容的追忆微笑，他坦然承认自己早年曾是一名坚定的社会民主党人。“不过我们这儿如今再也没有什么孟什维克了，”他急忙补充道；“早在很久以前，我们大家就集体集体加入了布尔什维克。”
“中央来的同志可能有所不知这其中的曲折内幕，”他的副手在一旁插话打趣道。“革命的现实往往会跟人开奇妙的幽默玩笑。你瞧，”他继续解释道，“当年外头有谣传风传莫斯科（Moscow）的孟什维克和社会革命党人已经全线阵前起义加入了布尔什维克。我们一合计，便决定紧跟步伐如法炮制。这就是为什么我们今天摇身一变成为了共产党员的缘由。不过那份传闻后来被证实纯属捕风捉影的假消息，”说到最后他的语气中透出了一丝淡淡的失落遗憾。
“但我们从未对此感到后悔过，”别钦语调沉稳斩钉截铁地总结道。
阿尔汉格尔斯克与摩尔曼斯克之间至今尚未修通直达的铁路干线，我们不得不踏上漫长艰辛的返程长途跋涉折返沃洛格达，方能辗转奔赴北冰洋沿岸。等辗转赶抵彼得罗扎沃茨克时，我们获悉由于遭遇了罕见的极度暴风雪肆虐封路，目前根本无法成行。今夜恰逢平安夜；按照此前的军令状誓言，我们必须在今年年底之前全员班师撤回彼得格勒（Petrograd）。万般无奈与痛心遗憾之下，我们不得不彻底放弃继续向极北地区挺进的考察计划。

## XXXVII Early Days of 1921 / XXXVII 1921年岁首
===Original===

The military fronts have been liquidated; civil war is at an end. The country breathes a sigh of relief. The Entente has ceased to finance counterrevolution, but the blockade still continues. It is now generally realized that the hope of near revolution in Europe is visionary. The proletariat of the West, involved in a severe struggle with growing reaction at home, can give no aid to Russia. The Soviet Republic is thrown upon its own resources.
All thoughts are turned to economic reconstruction. Communist circles and the official press are agitated by the discussion of the role of the workers in the present situation. It is admitted that militarization of labor has failed. Far from proving productive, as had been claimed, its effects have been disorganizing and demoralizing. The new part to be assigned to the proletariat is the burning problem, but there is no unity of opinion among the leading Bolsheviki. Lenin contends that the unions are not prepared to manage the industries: their main mission is to serve as “schools of Communism,” with gradually increasing participation in the economic field. Zinoviev and his following side with Lenin and elaborate his views. But Trotsky dissents, insisting that the workers will for a long time to come be unfit to manage the industries. He demands a “labor front,” subject to the iron discipline of a military campaign. In opposition to this conception, the labor elements advocate the immediate democratization of industrial government. The exclusion of the unions from a decisive role in the economic life, they maintain, is the true cause of the deplorable situation. They are confident that the revolutionary proletariat, who has defeated all armed opposition, will also conquer the enemy on the economic field. But the workers must be given the opportunity: they will learn by doing.
Throughout the country rages the discussion, on the solution of which depends the economic future of the people.
---
Many of the Anarchists arrested in Kharkov on the eve of the suppressed Conference have been brought to Moscow. Some of them are in the Butirki; others are held incommunicado in the “inner jail” of the Cheka. Volin, A. Baron, and Lea, the wife of my friend Yossif the Emigrant, are among them. Yossif is reported dead.

===Chinese===

各处军事战线已被彻底平定肃清；内战宣告硝烟散尽。饱经磨难的全国军民终于长舒了一口粗气。协约国已彻底停止向白卫军反革命提供财政军援，然而对苏俄的经济封锁依然如故。如今党内高层普遍清醒意识到，寄望于欧洲在短期内爆发无产阶级革命纯属脱离现实的虚幻空想。深陷于同本土日益猖獗的反动势力残酷博弈之中的西方无产阶级，根本无法腾出手向苏维埃俄国施以任何实质性援助。年轻的苏维埃共和国被迫彻底退回到依靠自身力量自力更生的严酷轨道之上。
全党全国的目光与思绪全面转向了国民经济恢复重建。在共产党高层核心圈子与官方报刊媒体上，围绕产业工人在当下历史关头究竟该扮演何种角色爆发了空前激烈的路线大争论。各方在私下里均不得不坦承，此前推行的劳动力军事化政策已经一败涂地。这项严苛举措非但未能如宣传预期的那样大幅提升社会生产力，反而给国民经济带来了毁灭性的秩序混乱与劳动者的意志消沉道德瓦解。赋予产业工人阶级何种全新历史使命，成为了当前最灼热烫手的焦点议题，然而布尔什维克领导层内部却根本无法达成任何思想共识。列宁坚称各工会目前根本尚未具备独立接管掌控现代工业生产的专业管理才能：工会最核心的历史使命应当是充当“共产主义的大学校”，通过循序渐进的方式逐步扩大其在经济领域的参与广度。季诺维也夫（Zinoviev）及其派系坚定倒向列宁一边，并进一步阐发充实了这一论纲。然而托洛茨基（Trotsky）却公开唱起反调，斩钉截铁断言工人在未来相当漫长的一段岁月里都绝不适宜执掌工业管理大权。他强硬要求必须打造一条坚不可摧的“劳动战线”，对工人们实行如军队战时冲锋陷阵般绝对严苛的铁血军法纪律。与这两种集权官僚观点形成鲜明对立的是，工运基层代表大声疾呼要求立刻推行工业民主化管理。他们义正词严地指出，正是由于将各工会彻底排挤排斥在国民经济核心决策大门之外，才直接酿成了今日惨绝人寰的经济凋敝苦局。他们满怀豪情地深信，曾经在战场上将一切武装敌人打得落花流水抱头鼠窜的革命无产阶级，在经济建设的主战场上同样能够战胜一切艰难险阻。但前提是国家必须放手赋予工人们大显身手的实践舞台：工人们必将在实践摸索中自学成才。
这场关乎全国亿万劳苦大众未来经济前途命运的大论战，正在全俄辽阔大地上如火如荼激烈展开。
---
在哈尔科夫（Kharkov）那场遭到当局武力查禁镇压的全国代表大会前夕被捕的大批无政府主义代表，如今已被秘密押解押送到了莫斯科（Moscow）。他们中有些人被囚禁在布特尔卡监狱（Butirki）；另一些人则被关押在契卡（the Cheka）的“内部监狱”中，被实行了彻底断绝外部联系的单独严苛监禁。沃林、A. 巴隆以及我的挚友移民约西夫（Yossif）的妻子莉娅赫然在列。而约西夫本人据传已不幸罹难身亡。

===Original===

With the consent of the Kharkov authorities, accompanied by two friends, he had gone to the Makhno camp to aid in arranging the conditions of agreement. On the way all three disappeared﻿—killed, it is assumed, just as they entered a village that was being pogromed. There are rumors of Bolshevik responsibility for the tragedy, but I cannot believe them guilty of such treachery.
By the aid of Angelica Balabanova we intercede in behalf of the victims of the broken truce between the Soviet Government and Makhno. Almost all of them come within the Communist definition of *ideini* Anarchists (of ideas); and Lenin had assured me that the Party is cordially disposed toward them. The efforts of Angelica have secured for me an appointment with Latsis, head of the “Department of Secret Operations” of the Veh-Cheka, in charge of the cases. But calling at the agreed hour, I am informed that Latsis, aware of the purpose of my visit, had left orders not to admit me.
Still believing in the possibility of establishing more amicable relations between the Soviet Government and the Anarchists, I appealed to influential Communists. The great task of rebuilding the country, I urge, necessitates mutual understanding and cooperation. But my Bolshevik friends scorn the suggestion as Utopian, though some of them are willing to aid in the liberation of certain individuals by serving as their “guarantee.” At last I decide to address myself to Lenin. In a written communication I present to him the situation and set forth the reasons﻿—revolutionary, ethical, and utilitarian﻿—for the release of the politicals in the interests of the common cause.
In vain I await a reply. The prison doors remain closed. More arrests take place in various parts of the country.
*February 13*﻿—Peter Kropotkin died on the 8th instant. Though not entirely unexpected, the news came to me as a great shock. I hastened from Petrograd to Dmitrov, where a number of personal friends of the dead man were already gathered. Almost the entire village accompanied the remains to the train bound for Moscow. Little children strewed the way with pine branches, and touching tribute was paid by the simple country folk to the man beloved in their midst.
The Soviet Government offered to take charge of the funeral, but the family of Kropotkin and his comrades have declined.

===Chinese===

在哈尔科夫苏维埃当局的红头公函首肯准许下，约西夫在两名挚友的陪同下，亲自奔赴马赫诺起义军营地协调落实双方同盟协定的和平条款。然而在半途中，这3人却神秘人间蒸发——据推测，他们极有可能是在刚刚踏入一座正惨遭匪徒血洗洗劫的村庄时不幸当场遇害。坊间甚至暗流涌动盛传布尔什维克特工策划暗杀了3人，但从良心出发，我绝不愿相信布尔什维克竟会自甘堕落犯下如此丧尽天良的背信弃义罪行。
在安杰丽卡·巴拉巴诺娃的大力协助斡旋下，我们出面为那场苏维埃政府与马赫诺停火同盟协议破裂的无辜政治受害者四处奔走呼吁请命。这批被捕人士几乎完全符合共产党官方定义中的“理念型无政府主义者”（ideini）；而列宁当年曾亲口向我拍胸脯保证，党对这批同志向来怀着最真诚亲切的阶级感情。在安杰丽卡的不懈奔走斡旋下，她好不容易为我争取到了全俄特肃委员会（契卡）主管此案的“秘密行动处”处长拉齐斯的当面会晤之约。然而，当我按照约定好的时间准时登门拜访时，却被冷冰冰地告知拉齐斯在获悉我此行的真实意图之后，早已下达了严禁放我进门的闭门逐客令。
我心底依然固执地相信在苏维埃政府与无政府主义阵营之间建立起更加和谐友好合作关系的可能性，于是我转而向党内几位极具政治影响力的布尔什维克显贵政要登门陈情。我苦口婆心地反复劝导，恢复重建千疮百孔的国民经济这项伟大历史伟业，迫切需要双方冰释前嫌相向而行精诚合作。然而我的布尔什维克朋友们却对我的这番良苦用心嗤之以鼻，讥讽这纯属脱离阶级斗争实际的乌托邦幼稚幻想，尽管他们中少数几位私下表示愿意以个人名誉作保出面“具保”，协助保释个别被捕私交好友。走投无路之下，我最终毅然决然决定直接致信列宁同志。在一封情真意切的长篇亲笔公函中，我详尽陈述了当前局势的原委脉络，并从革命大局、人类道德伦理以及实际效用三大维度深刻论证了为了两军联合的崇高共同事业而开释在押政治犯的历史紧迫性与必要性。
我在焦虑万分中苦苦守候，却迟迟未等来哪怕只字片语的回复。冰冷沉重的大牢铁门依旧紧闭如铁。而在全国各个省份，新一轮针对异见人士的秘密大抓捕却在紧锣密鼓地接连上演。
2月13日。——彼得·克鲁泡特金（Peter Kropotkin）不幸于本月8日与世长辞。尽管对噩耗早有心理准备，但这个惊天噩耗依然如晴天霹雳般令我整个人彻底陷入万分悲痛之中。我风尘仆仆从彼得格勒火速赶赴德米特罗夫（Dmitrov），只见死者生前的一众莫逆之交早已聚集在灵前守灵。送葬出殡之时，全村男女老少几乎倾巢而出，自发护送灵柩一路步行前往开往莫斯科的火车站。不谙世事的垂髫幼童将青翠的松枝一路铺撒在灵车途经的雪地泥土上，淳朴善良的农夫乡亲用这种朴素无华却催人泪下的深情仪式，向这位在他们村里隐居多年、深受大家由衷爱戴尊敬的长者表达了最后的崇高敬意。
苏维埃政府曾主动提出愿由国家全权包办治丧操办国葬，但遭到了克鲁泡特金家属及其生前亲密战友们的断然婉拒。

===Original===

They feel that Peter, who throughout his life denied the State, should not in death be insulted by its attentions. The Funeral Commission formed by the Moscow Anarchist organizations requested Lenin to permit the imprisoned comrades of the dead to attend the funeral of their friend and teacher. Lenin consented, and the Central Committee of the Party recommended to the Cheka the temporary release of the Anarchists. Delegated by the Commission to arrange the matter, I was given opportunity to visit the prisoners in the Butirki. Over a score of them gathered about me, pale, martyred faces, with eager eyes and palpitating interest in the life they are deprived of.
In the “inner jail” of the Cheka I was permitted to see A. Baron, the spokesman of the Anarchists imprisoned there. Accompanying me was Yartchuk, himself but recently released. “You’ll probably be with us again soon,” the magistrate remarked to him with a sardonic grimace.
Owing to the nationalization of all conveyances, printing facilities, and materials, the Commission has been compelled to apply to the Moscow Soviet to enable it to carry out the funeral program. After considerable delay, permission has been secured to issue a one-day, four-page in memoriam paper, but the authorities insist that the manuscripts﻿—though exclusively devoted to appreciations of Kropotkin as scholar, man, and Anarchist﻿—be submitted to censorship.
The Cheka refused to release the Anarchist prisoners without a guarantee of the Funeral Commission for their return. That guarantee secured, the Extraordinary Commission informed me that “upon examination it was found that there are no Anarchists that could be freed.” There followed an intensive exchange of “opinions” between the Moscow Soviet, in the person of Kamenev, its president, and the Cheka, which has finally resulted in the solemn promise to permit all the imprisoned Moscow Anarchists to attend the funeral.
In the Hall of Columns in the Union House the remains of Peter Kropotkin lie in state. A continuous procession of workers, students, and peasants passes before the bier to pay the last tribute to the dead. Outside a vast mass is waiting to accompany the remains to their resting place. All is in readiness; the requiem has been sung, the time set for starting has already passed. But the Anarchist prisoners have not come.

===Chinese===

在大家看来，克鲁泡特金毕生都在用全部心血与思想理论坚决否定国家强权国家主义，在他溘然长逝之后，绝不应在九泉之下再遭受来自国家强权假惺惺官方关怀的莫大亵渎。由莫斯科各大无政府主义团体紧急联合组建的民间治丧委员会致信列宁，恳请特许批准那些仍在铁窗囹圄中身陷大牢的死者生前战友们走出牢房，亲自出席这位生前导师兼挚友的出殡葬礼。列宁当场签署了特批公函，党的中央委员会随即正式责令契卡（the Cheka）临时保释开释在押的无政府主义囚犯。受治丧委员会全权委托负责落实交涉此事，我得以获得罕见的破格特许，亲自步入阴森恐怖的布尔什维克布特尔卡监狱探视在押同志。20多位同志迅速围拢在我的身旁，他们一个个面容苍白枯槁宛如殉道烈士，然而一双双眼眸中却闪烁着炽热的光芒，对被强行剥夺的外面大千世界展现出了急切而激动的强烈渴望。
在契卡戒备森严的“内部监狱”里，我被获准探视接见在押无政府主义者的总发言人A. 巴隆。陪同我一同前往的还有刚刚重获自由走出地牢的亚尔丘克。在擦肩而过时，契卡审判长嘴角勾起一抹阴森刻毒的冷笑，冷嘲热讽地挖苦亚尔丘克道：“要不了多久，保准你又得乖乖搬回咱们这里来常住啦！”
由于全国所有的交通运输工具、印刷印刷厂以及新闻纸张物资早已被国家全额没收收归国有，治丧委员会不得不硬着头皮向莫斯科苏维埃打报告呈递公文，以便落实治丧大典的筹备工作。在经历了一连串旷日持久的官僚审批踢皮球之后，当局终于勉强恩准特许发行一期四版的悼念纪念特刊，但书报审查机关蛮横坚持：所有文稿——哪怕通篇纯粹是对克鲁泡特金作为卓越学者、崇高伟人以及无政府主义泰斗的学术品格盖棺定论——也必须无一例外全盘呈报接受严苛的政治思想书报审查。
契卡断然拒绝放人，强硬勒令治丧委员会必须白纸黑字签署全额连带人身归监担保书。即便这一屈辱的担保文书全部签署办妥之后，特肃委员会依然傲慢地通知我，“经政治审查甄别，发现大牢里根本没有任何符合保释开释条件的无政府主义罪犯”。紧接着，以主席加米涅夫（Kamenev）为首的莫斯科苏维埃与契卡之间爆发了一场剑拔弩张的唇枪舌剑内部公函较量，最终在各方高压博弈之下，契卡才终于庄严立下字据许诺，准许所有被关押在莫斯科境内的无政府主义政治犯集体出狱出席葬礼。
在工会大厦宏伟的圆柱大厅里，彼得·克鲁泡特金的遗体静卧在鲜花灵柩之中接受国葬级瞻仰。由产业工人、青年大学生与淳朴农民汇聚而成的川流不息的肃穆人流，缓缓从灵柩前肃穆走过，向这位长眠的伟人致以最后的崇高告别。大厦外的广场上，汪洋大海般的庞大送葬人潮在凛冽寒风中肃立等候，准备护送遗体前往长眠的墓地。一切就绪；安魂曲已经唱罢，原定的起棺吉时早已过去良久。然而，契卡关押的大批无政府主义战友们却始终不见踪影。

===Original===

Urgent inquiries at the Cheka elicit contradictory information: the collective guarantee of the Commission is not satisfactory, we are told﻿—the men refuse to attend the funeral﻿—they have already been released.
It is past noon. The funeral is delayed. It is apparent that the Cheka is sabotaging our agreement. We decide to protest by demonstratively removing the Government and Communist wreaths from the Hall. The threat of a public scandal quickly brings the authorities to terms, and within a quarter of an hour the seven prisoners of the “inner jail” arrive. We are assured that the Butirki Anarchists have been freed and will presently join us.50
The long procession slowly winds its way to the cemetery. The students, arms linked, form a living chain on both sides of the great multitude. The sun is bright upon the hard, glistening snow. Black Anarchist banners, interspersed with flashes of scarlet, flutter above like mournful arms of love.
A Kropotkin Museum is to be founded in memory of the great Anarchist scientist and teacher. The committee of Anarchist organizations in charge of the undertaking has requested Emma Goldman and myself to aid in its organization. Our further cooperation with the Museum of the Revolution has become impossible owing to the arbitrary attitude of the Ispart. Moreover, the Kropotkin work is of greater immediate importance and of stronger appeal to my sympathies. I have severed my connection with the Museum to accept the secretaryship of the Kropotkin Memorial Commission.

===Chinese===

向契卡拍发的加急查询换来的全是一连串自相矛盾、荒诞不经的说辞：一会儿推诿称治丧委员会出具的集体保释担保书不合规范——一会儿又宣称是犯人们自己思想抵触拒绝出席葬礼——一会儿甚至公然撒谎谎称人早就已经全部开释出狱了。
时针早已指过了正午。葬礼出殡被迫严重延宕延误。残酷的事实再清楚不过：契卡正在处心积虑背信弃义暗中全面破坏此前达成的停火释放协议。忍无可忍之下，我们当机立断决定采取最强硬的公开抗议姿态：当着全世界新闻媒体与全场送葬群众的面，当众把工会大厦灵堂内悬挂的所有苏维埃官方与共产党的红白悼念花圈全部强行摘除撤出灵堂！这记掷地有声的公开政治决裂恐吓瞬间击中了官僚当局的软肋，生怕激起全城群众暴力民愤的当局立刻乖乖妥协就范；不到15分钟内，被关押在契卡“内部监狱”里的7名核心骨干同志便被荷枪实弹火速押解护送到了灵堂现场。当局信誓旦旦向我们立字据担保，布特尔卡监狱的无政府主义大队人马此刻也已全部获释出狱，马上就会赶来同大部队胜利汇合50。
蜿蜒如长龙般的送葬浩瀚队伍迈着沉重肃穆的步伐，缓缓向墓地蠕动挺进。青年大学生们手挽着手，在浩浩荡荡的大军两侧筑起了一道长达数里的人体血肉警卫护卫长城。冬日骄阳洒在坚硬如铁、银光闪烁的皑皑雪原之上。迎风猎猎作响的黑色无政府主义大纛战旗，与零星闪烁其间的鲜红战旗交相辉映，在寒风中宛如死者在天之灵伸出的无数双充满哀思与眷恋的慈爱臂弯在空中剧烈翻飞。
为深切缅怀这位功勋卓著的无政府主义科学泰斗与伟大导师，各界一致决定筹建一座克鲁泡特金纪念博物馆。全权执掌这一崇高历史伟业的无政府主义联合筹备委员会正式恳请爱玛·戈尔德曼与我出面担纲核心组织筹建工作。鉴于俄共（布）党史委员会飞扬跋扈的专横跋扈做派，我们同彼得格勒十月革命博物馆的进一步深入合作在事实上早已破裂不可能维系下去了。更何况，全身心投入克鲁泡特金博物馆的抢救性筹建工作，在当下不仅具有十万火急的重大历史现实紧迫性，更深深契合我内心的道义良知与全部情感共鸣。我毅然决然斩断了同革命博物馆的一切官方人事从属瓜葛，郑重受命履新出任克鲁泡特金纪念筹备委员会秘书长一职。

## XXXVIII Kronstadt / XXXVIII 喀琅施塔得
===Original===

*Petrograd, February, 1921.*﻿—The cold is extreme and there is intense suffering in the city. Snowstorms have isolated us from the provinces; the supply of provisions has almost ceased. Only half a pound of bread is being issued now. Most of the houses are unheated. At dusk old women prowl about the big woodpile near the Hotel Astoria, but the sentry is vigilant. Several factories have been closed for lack of fuel, and the employees put on half rations. They called a meeting to consult about the situation, but the authorities did not permit it to take place.
The Trubotchny millworkers have gone on strike. In the distribution of winter clothing, they complain, the Communists received undue advantage over the nonpartisans. The Government refuses to consider the grievances till the men return to work.
Crowds of strikers gathered in the street near the mills, and soldiers were sent to disperse them. They were *kursanti*, Communist youths of the military academy. There was no violence.
Now the strikers have been joined by the men from the Admiralty shops and Galernaya docks. There is much resentment against the arrogant attitude of the Government. A street demonstration was attempted, but mounted troops suppressed it.
*February 27.*﻿—Nervous feeling in the city. The strike situation is growing more serious. The Patronny mills, the Baltiysky and Laferm factories have suspended operations. The authorities have ordered the strikers to resume work. Martial law in the city. The special Committee of Defense (Komitet Oboroni) is vested with exceptional powers, Zinoviev at its head.
At the Soviet session last evening a military member of the Defense Committee denounced the strikers as traitors to the Revolution. It was Lashevitch. He looked fat, greasy, and offensively sensuous. He called the dissatisfied workers “leeches attempting extortion” (*shkurniki*), and demanded drastic measures against them. The Soviet passed a resolution locking out the men of the Trubotchny mill. It means deprivation of rations﻿—actual starvation.
*February 28.*﻿—Strikers’ proclamations have appeared on the streets today. They cite cases of workers found frozen to death in their homes. The main demand is for winter clothing and more regular issue of rations. Some of the circulars protest against the suppression of factory meetings.

===Chinese===

彼得格勒（Petrograd），1921年2月。——天气酷寒彻骨，全城百姓在苦难中苦苦煎熬。漫天暴风雪彻底阻断了我们与外省的交通联系；粮食副食品供应几乎全面告罄停顿。如今每人每天仅配发半磅（半磅）面包。绝大多数民居没有半点暖气。暮色降临之际，老妇人们像幽灵般在阿斯托里亚酒店（Hotel Astoria）近旁的高大木料堆周围四处徘徊寻找机会，但持枪的哨兵警惕地来回巡视。数家工厂因严重缺乏燃料燃料而被迫停产关门，职工的配给指标被直接腰斩减半。工人们召集集会商讨应对危局，但当局严厉勒令取缔严禁集会举行。
特鲁博奇内工厂（Trubotchny）的工人们举行了罢工。工人们愤愤不平地申诉抗议道，在冬令防寒棉衣的配发上，共产党员获得了远优于非党群众的不公特权。政府断然拒绝受理申诉诉求，强硬勒令工人必须无条件全员复工。
大批罢工工人聚集在厂区附近的街道上，当局紧急派遣军队前往驱散弹压。那是军校学员（kursanti）——军事学院的年轻共产党员。现场并未发生流血暴力冲突。
如今，海军造船厂与加勒纳亚码头的产业工人也纷纷起而响应加入了罢工行列。面对政府傲慢跋扈的强硬态度，工人们满腔义愤。群众曾试图发动街头示威游行，但遭到骑兵部队的当场暴力弹压。
2月27日。——全城弥漫着极度神经质的紧绷恐慌。罢工风暴愈演愈烈日趋白热化。弹药制造厂、波罗的海造船厂以及拉费姆卷烟厂均已停工停产。当局下达死命令勒令罢工者即刻复工。全城宣布戒严。以季诺维也夫（Zinoviev）为首的特设防御委员会（Komitet Oboroni）被授予了至高无上的非凡全权。
在昨晚举行的彼得格勒苏维埃大会上，防御委员会的一位军方委员当场指斥罢工工人是背叛革命的叛徒。此人正是拉舍维奇（Lashevitch）。他脑满肠肥、油头粉面，举手投足间散发着令人作呕的贪婪肉欲。他恶毒辱骂心怀不满的工人们是“企图趁火打劫敲诈勒索的吸血水蛭/自私自利者（shkurniki）”，并声嘶力竭要求对他们施以铁腕严惩。苏维埃随即表决通过了一项对特鲁博奇内工厂工人实行全员关厂开除的严酷决议。这意味着直接剥夺这批工人的全部口粮配给——无异于蓄意制造赤裸裸的人为饥荒。
2月28日。——今天大街小巷赫然贴出了罢工工人的传单宣言。传单列举了多起工人在自己家中被活活冻死的骇人惨剧。工人们的核心诉求是要求发放防寒棉衣并更为稳定规律地配发救命口粮。部分传单还就当局粗暴查禁工厂群众集会提出了强烈抗议。

===Original===

“The people want to take counsel together to find means of relief,” they state. Zinoviev asserts the whole trouble is due to Menshevik and Social Revolutionist plotting.
For the first time a political turn is being given to the strikes. Late in the afternoon a proclamation was posted containing larger demands. “A complete change is necessary in the policies of the Government,” it reads. “First of all, the workers and peasants need freedom. They don’t want to live by the decrees of the Bolsheviki; they want to control their own destinies. We demand the liberation of all arrested Socialists and nonpartisan workingmen; abolition of martial law; freedom of speech, press, and assembly for all who labor; free election of shop and factory committees, of labor union and Soviet representatives.”
*March 1.*﻿—Many arrests are taking place. Groups of strikers surrounded by Chekists, on their way to prison, are a common sight. Much indignation in the city. I hear that several unions have been liquidated and their active members turned over to the Cheka. But proclamations continue to appear. The arbitrary stand of the authorities is having the effect of rousing reactionary tendencies. The situation is growing tense. Calls for the Utchredilka (Constituent Assembly) are being heard. A manifesto is circulating, signed by the “Socialist Workers of the Nevsky District,” openly attacking the Communist regime. “We know who is afraid of the Constituent Assembly,” it declares. “It is they who will no longer be able to rob us. Instead they will have to answer before the representatives of the people for their deceit, their thefts, and all their crimes.”
Zinoviev is alarmed; he has wired to Moscow for troops. The local garrison is said to be in sympathy with the strikers. Military from the provinces has been ordered to the city: special Communist regiments have already arrived. Extraordinary martial law has been declared today.
*March 2.*﻿—Most disquieting reports. Large strikes have broken out in Moscow. In the Astoria I heard today that armed conflicts have taken place near the Kremlin and blood has been shed. The Bolsheviki claim the coincidence of events in the two capitals as proof of a counterrevolutionary conspiracy.
It is said that Kronstadt sailors have come to the city to look into the cause of trouble. Impossible to tell fact from fiction.

===Chinese===

“人民渴望围坐在一起共商良策寻觅自救之道，”传单上赫然写道。季诺维也夫却一口咬定整场风波纯属孟什维克与社会革命党人处心积虑策划的反动阴谋。
罢工潮有史以来第一次被赋予了鲜明的政治色彩。傍晚时分，街头张贴出了一份提出更高政治诉求的震撼宣言。“政府的现行大政方针必须实行彻底根本性的改弦更张，”宣言写道。“首当其冲的是，工人和农民迫切需要自由。人民绝不甘心继续苟活在布尔什维克一纸纸蛮横法令的严苛羁绊之下；人民渴望亲自掌控主宰自身的历史命运。我们严正要求：无条件释放所有被捕的社会主义者与非党工运同志；立即废除全城戒严令；赋予全体劳动人民以绝对的言论、出版与集会结社自由；由车间与工矿基层工人和群众自由民主选举工厂委员会、工会理事会以及苏维埃人民代表！”
3月1日。——全城正在大肆搜捕抓人。大批罢工群众在契卡（the Cheka）特工荷枪实弹的押解下押往阴森大牢的惨景，在街头巷尾随处可见。全城上下群情激愤怨声载道。我获悉数家工会已被当局强行清算解散，其骨干活跃积极分子已被全数移交契卡秘密审讯。然而，反对派的抗议传单依然层出不穷地在街头涌现。当局的专横独裁手腕反而激化煽动起了强烈的反弹敌意倾向。局势正在变得空前剑拔弩张。要求重新召开全俄立宪会议（Utchredilka）的呼声在民间不胫而走。一份由“涅瓦区社会主义工人”署名的联合宣言正在地下秘密广泛流传，公开向共产党的独裁专制政权发难。“我们心里明镜似的究竟是谁在恐惧立宪会议，”宣言义正词严地宣告道。“正是那些妄图继续骑在人民头上作威作福抢劫搜刮我们的人！相反，他们必须在人民真正选出的合法代表面前，为他们犯下的弥天欺骗、巧取豪夺以及一切血腥罪行接受历史的庄严审判！”
季诺维也夫惊恐万状；他已紧急致电莫斯科（Moscow）火速调兵遣将请求重兵增援。据说本地驻军在情感上普遍同情支持罢工工人的正义诉求。来自外省的精锐正规军已被紧急下令开进彼得格勒：共产党的特别嫡系警卫团已先期开抵。今天全城正式宣布进入特级戒严状态。
3月2日。——前线传来最令人坐卧不安的严峻战报。莫斯科爆发了空前浩大的工人总罢工。在阿斯托里亚酒店里，我今天惊悉克里姆林宫附近爆发了武装流血冲突，街头已血流成河。布尔什维克将两座革命之都同时爆发的事态巧合，作为存在反革命联合大阴谋铁证如山的借口。
坊间盛传喀琅施塔得（Kronstadt）的水兵们已秘密派代表进城，实地微服私访查明这场工潮风暴的真实起因。在谣言四起的混乱之中，真伪莫辨。

===Original===

The absence of a public press encourages the wildest rumors. The official papers are discredited.
*March 3.*﻿—Kronstadt is disturbed. It disapproves of the Government’s drastic methods against the dissatisfied workers. The men of the warship *Petropavlovsk* have passed a resolution of sympathy with the strikers.
It has become known today that on February 28 a committee of sailors was sent to this city to investigate the strike situation. Its report was unfavorable to the authorities. On March 1 the crews of the First and Second Squadrons of the Baltic Fleet called a public meeting at Yakorny Square. The gathering was attended by sixteen thousand sailors, Red Army men, and workers. The Chairman of the Executive Committee of the Kronstadt Soviet, the Communist Vassiliev, presided. The audience was addressed by Kalinin, President of the Republic, and by Kuzmin, Commissar of the Baltic Fleet. The attitude of the sailors was entirely friendly to the Soviet Government, and Kalinin was met on his arrival in Kronstadt with military honors, music, and banners.
At the meeting the Petrograd situation and the report of the sailors’ investigating committee were discussed. The audience was outspoken in its indignation at the means employed by Zinoviev against the workers. President Kalinin and Commissar Kusmin berated the strikers and denounced the Petropavlovsk Resolution as counterrevolutionary. The sailors emphasized their loyalty to the Soviet system, but condemned the Bolshevik bureaucracy. The resolution was passed.
*March 4.*﻿—Great nervous tension in the city. The strikes continue; labor disorders have again taken place in Moscow. A wave of discontent is sweeping the country. Peasant uprisings are reported from Tambov, Siberia, the Ukraine, and Caucasus. The country is on the verge of desperation. It was confidently hoped that with the end of civil war the Communists would mitigate the severe military regime. The Government had announced its intention of economic reconstruction, and the people were eager to cooperate. They looked forward to the lightening of the heavy burdens, the abolition of wartime restrictions, and the introduction of elemental liberties.
The fronts are liquidated, but the old policies continue, and labor militarization is paralyzing industrial revival.

===Chinese===

独立公共报刊的全面被剥夺取缔，为形形色色荒诞离奇的恐怖谣言大肆蔓延推波助澜。官方喉舌报纸早已威信扫地无人问津。
3月3日。——喀琅施塔得军港彻底震动沸腾了。水兵们对政府针对心怀不满的饥民工人施以的血腥强权手腕深恶痛绝坚决反对。“彼得保罗”号战舰（Petropavlovsk）的水兵们全票表决通过了一项声援声讨罢工工人的正义决议案。
今天外界获悉，早在2月28日，水兵们便秘密指派了一个调查代表团深入彼得格勒全城，就罢工危机真相展开实地独立调查。代表团带回的调查报告对苏维埃官僚当局极为不利。3月1日，波罗的海舰队第一分舰队与第二分舰队的水兵全体官兵在锚地广场（Yakorny Square）隆重召开了水兵群众大会。整整16000名水兵、红军战士和造船工人出席了这场规模空前的誓师大会。喀琅施塔得苏维埃执行委员会主席、共产党员瓦西里耶夫亲自主持大会。全俄苏维埃共和国最高主席加里宁、以及波罗的海舰队特派政委库兹明先后在大会上发表了重要讲话。水兵们起初对苏维埃政权抱有极其友善真诚的阶级感情，加里宁抵达喀琅施塔得军港码头时，受到了军乐高奏、彩旗飘扬的隆重最高军礼检阅迎接。
在大会上，代表们对彼得格勒工运严峻危局以及水兵调查团的详尽报告展开了热烈审议。与会军民群情激愤，对季诺维也夫针对劳苦工人的残暴手段进行了公开的严厉谴责抨击。加里宁主席与库兹明政委劈头盖脸痛斥罢工工人，声嘶力竭辱骂“彼得保罗”号决议案是不折不扣的反革命纲领。水兵们慷慨陈词重申自己对真正苏维埃民主制度的无限忠诚，但坚决谴责布尔什维克官僚体制的专制腐朽。决议案最终以压倒性多数全票通过。
3月4日。——整座城市陷入了高度神经质的惊恐战栗。罢工浪潮仍在汹涌蔓延；莫斯科再度爆发了严重的工运骚乱工潮。不满与抗争的狂潮正席卷全俄大江南北。来自坦波夫、西伯利亚、乌克兰（Ukraine）以及高加索地区的农民武装暴动烽火连天。整个国家正滑向绝望的万丈深渊。全国人民此前曾满怀豪情地深信，随着内战残酷战事的硝烟散尽，共产党人必定会顺应民心逐步放宽严苛的战时军事管制。苏维埃政府曾向全世界庄严宣告经济和平重建的施政大计，劳苦大众曾万分渴望携手同心大干一场。人民翘首期盼着减免身上沉重的摊派枷锁、彻底废除战时严酷禁令，并重新赢得最基本的人身自由与民主权利。
前线战火虽已熄灭，然而旧日的极权老政策却依然如故变本加厉，残酷的劳动力军事化政策正在将国民经济与工业复苏的生机彻底扼杀瘫痪。

===Original===

It is openly charged that the Communist Party is more interested in entrenching its political power than in saving the Revolution.
An official manifesto appeared today. It is signed by Lenin and Trotsky and declares Kronstadt guilty of mutiny (*myatezh*). The demand of the sailors for free Soviets is denounced as “a counterrevolutionary conspiracy against the proletarian Republic.” Members of the Communist Party are ordered into the mills and factories to “rally the workers to the support of the Government against the traitors.” Kronstadt is to be suppressed.
The Moscow radio station sent out a message addressed “to all, all, all”:

> Petrograd is orderly and quiet, and even the few factories where accusations against the Soviet Government were recently voiced now understand that it is the work of provocators﻿ ﻿… just at this moment, when in America a new Republican regime is assuming the reins of government and showing inclination to take up business relations with Soviet Russia, the spreading of lying rumors and the organization of disturbances in Kronstadt have the sole purpose of influencing the American President and changing his policy toward Russia. At the same time the London Conference is holding its sessions, and the spreading of similar rumors must influence also the Turkish delegation and make it more submissive to the demands of the Entente. The rebellion of the *Petropavlovask* crew is undoubtedly part of a great conspiracy to create trouble within Soviet Russia and to injure our international position.﻿ ﻿… This plan is being carried out within Russia by a Tsarist general and former officers, and their activities are supported by the Mensheviki and Social Revolutionists.

===Chinese===

民间已公开指责共产党（the Communist Party）满脑子想的只是如何不择手段巩固垄断其一党私利政治强权，而根本毫无拯救十月革命成果的半点诚意。
今天官方正式出台了一份火药味十足的严厉宣言。这份由列宁与托洛茨基（Trotsky）联袂亲笔签署的最后通牒，公然宣布喀琅施塔得犯下了武装叛乱罪（myatezh）。水兵们关于实现真正自由民主选举苏维埃的正义呼声，被恶毒污蔑为“颠覆无产阶级共和国的反革命弥天阴谋”。当局严令全体共产党员火速进驻各大工矿企业车间厂房，负责“动员组织全体工人坚决支持苏维埃政府平定这帮反革命叛徒”。喀琅施塔得已注定要遭到武力血洗弹压。
莫斯科广播电台向全世界播发了一份标题为“致全俄全体军民乃至全世界人民”的官方公告：
> “彼得格勒全城秩序井然、风平浪静，甚至少数几家前几日曾对苏维埃政府发表过激指责言论的个别工厂，如今也已彻底清醒意识到那纯属阶级敌人密探挑衅分子的险恶捣鬼……恰逢此时此刻，美利坚合众国新一届共和党新政府刚刚上台执政、并主动展现出渴望同苏维埃俄国恢复发展双边正常经贸往来的关键历史节点，在喀琅施塔得大肆散布耸人听闻的弥天谎言谣言、策划武装暴乱骚乱，其唯一罪恶图谋无非是企图借机向美国新总统施加政治压力、迫使其改弦更张恶化对俄方针。与此同时，伦敦和会正在紧锣密鼓召开，散布此类反动谣言势必严重动摇恐吓土耳其代表团立场、迫使其向协约国帝国主义的讹诈全面屈膝妥协。‘彼得保罗’号舰员的武装叛乱，毫无疑问是国际反动势力企图在苏俄内部挑起内乱、蓄意重创我们国际政治地位的庞大反动反革命阴谋的核心组成部分……这项罪恶图谋在俄国内部正由一名沙皇旧将与一批旧军官亲自策划指挥，而孟什维克与社会革命党人正是他们鞍前马后甘当帮凶走狗的反动羽翼。”

===Original===

The whole Northern District is under martial law and all gatherings are interdicted. Elaborate precautions have been taken to protect the Government institutions. Machine guns are placed in the Astoria, the living quarters of Zinoviev and other prominent Bolsheviki. These preparations are increasing general nervousness. Official proclamations command the immediate return of the strikers to the factories, prohibit suspension of work, and warn the populace against congregating in the streets.
The Committee of Defense has initiated a “cleaning” of the city. Many workers suspected of sympathizing with Kronstadt have been placed under arrest. All Petrograd sailors and part of the garrison thought to be “untrustworthy” have been ordered to distant points, while the families of Kronstadt sailors living in Petrograd are held as hostages. The Committee of Defense notified Kronstadt that “the prisoners are kept as ‘pledges’ for the safety of the Commissar of the Baltic Fleet, N. N. Kusmin, the Chairman of the Kronstadt Soviet, T. Vassiliev, and other Communists. If the least harm be suffered by our comrades, the hostages will pay with their lives.”
“We want no bloodshed,” Kronstadt wired in reply. “Not a single Communist has been harmed by us.”
The Petrograd workers are anxiously awaiting developments. They hope that the intercession of the sailors may turn the situation in their favor. The term of office of the Kronstadt Soviet is about to expire, and arrangements are being made for the coming elections.
On March 2 a conference of delegates took place, at which three hundred representatives of the ships, the garrison, the labor unions and factories were present, among them also a number of Communists. The Conference approved the Resolution passed by the mass-meeting the previous day. Lenin and Trotsky have declared it counterrevolutionary and proof of a White conspiracy.52

> Having heard the report of the representatives sent by the General Meeting of Ship Crews to Petrograd to investigate the situation there, Resolved:

===Chinese===

整个北方军区宣布进入最高战备戒严状态，严禁民间一切形式的群众集会。当局采取了极其森严周密的战备预防措施以严密拱卫各苏维埃政府衙门机关。重机枪工事被直接架设在阿斯托里亚酒店的大门口与楼顶——那里正是季诺维也夫以及其他布尔什维克高级显贵们的秘密私邸寓所。这些大动干戈的肃杀军事戒备更加剧了全城百姓如临大敌的极度恐慌不安。官方布告严令罢工群众即刻无条件返回各厂区工作岗位，明令禁止任何擅自停工怠工，并严厉警告全城居民严禁在街头聚众逗留。
防御委员会（Komitet Oboroni）全面启动了针对全城的“大清洗大整肃运动”。大批被怀疑暗中同情支持喀琅施塔得水兵的产业工人遭到了秘密逮捕投入大牢。彼得格勒所有的驻防海军水兵以及被定性为“政治不可靠”的大批陆军驻防部队被连夜强行遣送发配到遥远偏远外省前线，而常住生活在彼得格勒境内的喀琅施塔得水兵家属亲眷则被契卡全数扣押充当人质。防御委员会向喀琅施塔得军民发出了赤裸裸的人质通牒，声称：“被扣押的全部直系亲属将被作为确保波罗的海舰队政委N. N. 库兹明同志、喀琅施塔得苏维埃主席T. 瓦西里耶夫同志以及其他在押共产党员人身绝对安全的‘抵押人质’。一旦我们的同志受到哪怕一根毫毛的闪失伤害，在押人质将立刻全额以死抵命！”
“我们决不愿妄动干戈涂炭生灵，”喀琅施塔得前线拍发回电掷地有声驳斥道。“在我们手中，没有哪怕一名共产党员受到过丝毫虐待伤害。”
彼得格勒的广大产业工人们正怀着焦灼万分的心情密切关注着战局的最新演变。他们心底寄望着水兵们强硬有力的正义挺身介入，能够力挽狂澜将严峻危局彻底扭转为有利于工人们的一方。喀琅施塔得苏维埃的本届法定任期即将届满，新一届苏维埃的筹备选举换届工作正在紧锣密鼓地周密组织展开。
3月2日，一场全军全民代表大会隆重召开，来自各战舰水兵、陆地驻防守军部队、各工会以及工矿企业的300名正式代表出席了大会，其中也包括相当数量的共产党员代表。代表大会郑重表决批准了前一天全军群众大会通过的历史性决议案。而列宁与托洛茨基却蛮横地将其定性为反革命叛乱纲领与白卫军阴谋的铁证52。
> “在听取了战舰舰员全体大会特派赴彼得格勒实地调查当地工潮真相的代表团的调查详报之后，大会郑重决议如下：”

===Original===

*March 4.*﻿—Late at night. The extraordinary session of the Petro-Soviet in the Tauride Palace was packed with Communists, mostly youngsters, fanatical and intolerant. Admission by special ticket; a *propusk* (permit) also had to be secured to return home after interdicted hours. Representatives of shops and labor committees were in the galleries, the seats in the main body having been occupied by Communists. Some factory delegates were given the floor, but the moment they attempted to state their case, they were shouted down. Zinoviev repeatedly urged the meeting to give the opposition an opportunity to be heard, but his appeal lacked energy and conviction.
Not a voice was raised in favor of the Constituent Assembly. A millworker pleaded with the Government to consider the complaints of the workers who are cold and hungry. Zinoviev replied that the strikers are enemies of the Soviet regime. Kalinin declared Kronstadt the headquarters of General Kozlovsky’s plot. A sailor reminded Zinoviev of the time when he and Lenin were hunted as counterrevolutionists by Kerensky and were saved by the very sailors whom they now denounce as traitors. Kronstadt demands only honest elections, he declared. He was not allowed to proceed. The stentorian voice and impassioned appeal of Yevdakimov, Zinoviev’s lieutenant, wrought the Communists up to a high pitch of excitement. His resolution was passed amid a tumult of protest from the nonpartisan delegates and labor men. The resolution declared Kronstadt guilty of a counterrevolutionary attempt against the Soviet regime and demands its immediate surrender. It is a declaration of war.
*March 5.*﻿—Many Bolsheviki refuse to believe that the Soviet resolution will be carried out. It were too monstrous a thing to attack by force of arms the “pride and glory of the Russian Revolution,” as Trotsky christened the Kronstadt sailors. In the circle of their friends many Communists threaten to resign from the Party should such a bloody deed come to pass.
Trotsky was to address the Petro-Soviet last evening. His failure to appear was interpreted as indicating that the seriousness of the situation has been exaggerated. But during the night he arrived and today he issued an ultimatum to Kronstadt:

===Chinese===

3月4日。——深夜。在塔夫利宫召开的彼得格勒苏维埃非常扩大会议大厅里，挤满了狂热盲从、专横跋扈的年轻布尔什维克党徒。与会者必须凭特批请柬入场；若想在全城宵禁戒严的严苛宵禁时间过后安全回家，还必须千方百计办妥一张特种夜间通行证（propusk）。来自各厂矿车间与工会工委会的基层工人代表被冷酷地排挤发配在二楼偏僻狭窄的旁听挑台走廊上，而一层主会场最宽敞体面的核心坐席则被共产党员们霸占瓜分殆尽。几位产业工人代表好不容易获得发言机会，但只要他们一试图陈述基层疾苦与工人诉求，台下立刻爆发出雷鸣般铺天盖地的狂暴喝倒彩叫骂声将其粗暴打断淹没。季诺维也夫数度虚情假意地假惺惺呼吁全场保持克制、应当给反对派一个陈述政见的机会，但他的做作言辞中毫无半点真诚力度与政治担当。
整场大会没有任何一个人站出来呼吁支持召开立宪会议。一名纺织工人代表声泪俱下地苦苦哀求苏维埃政府能够俯察民情，体恤体恤那些正在刺骨饥寒中苦苦挣扎求生的苦难工人们。季诺维也夫却冷酷无情地当场定性斥责罢工工人是苏维埃政权的阶级死敌。加里宁则煞有介事地大肆污蔑宣称喀琅施塔得早已沦为了白军将领科兹洛夫斯基将军策划军事政变的匪巢大本营。一名大义凛然的海军水兵当场拍案而起厉声喝斥季诺维也夫，当众提醒他切莫忘记当年他和列宁被克伦斯基反动政权以反革命罪名满世界悬赏缉拿时，恰恰正是今天被他们污蔑为叛徒的这帮喀琅施塔得水兵冒死将他们从枪口下营救保全下来的！水兵义正词严地高呼，喀琅施塔得的全部诉求唯有要求举行干干净净、公正廉洁的苏维埃民主选举！然而他的讲话被军警粗暴扼杀打断剥夺了发言权。季诺维也夫的得力干将叶夫多基莫夫随即登台，以雷霆万钧的洪亮大嗓门发表了一番煽动性极强的狂热政治演说，将全场共产党员的盲目亢奋情绪煽动到了失控的沸点。在他一手操弄下，伴随着非党工人代表席上爆发出的滔天抗议怒吼，大会强行表决通过了一项决议。该决议公然宣布喀琅施塔得犯下了颠覆苏维埃政权的反革命暴乱死罪，严令其必须立刻无条件缴械投降。这无异于一封赤裸裸的同胞自相残杀的公开宣战书！
3月5日。——党内大批老布尔什维克同志依然拒绝相信苏维埃这项决议真会付诸武力付诸东流。毕竟，拿起枪炮去公开屠杀托洛茨基当年亲口盛赞为“俄罗斯革命的骄傲与无上光荣”的喀琅施塔得英雄水兵，在伦理道德上简直是一桩丧心病狂、耸人听闻的滔天罪孽！在私下密友圈子里，大批有良知的共产党员公开拍桌子扬言，倘若这种同胞骨肉相残的血腥屠杀真敢悍然上演，他们将当场撕毁党证退出共产党！
托洛茨基原定昨晚亲临彼得格勒苏维埃发表公开施政演说。他的突然爽约缺席，在民间一度被乐观解读为局势的危急程度或许被外界人为夸大夸张了。然而，就在昨夜深更半夜，这位战争狂人终于秘密抵城，并于今天清晨以雷霆万钧之势正式向喀琅施塔得下达了最后的死刑通牒：

===Original===

> The Workers’ and Peasants’ Government has decreed that Kronstadt and the rebellious ships must immediately submit to the authority of the Soviet Republic. Therefore, I command all who have raised their hand against the Socialist fatherland to lay down their arms at once. The obdurate are to be disarmed and turned over to the Soviet authorities. The arrested Commissars and other representatives of the Government are to be liberated at once. Only those surrendering unconditionally may count on the mercy of the Soviet Republic.
> Simultaneously I am issuing orders to prepare to quell the mutiny and subdue the mutineers by force of arms. Responsibility for the harm that may be suffered by the peaceful population will fall entirely upon the heads of the counterrevolutionary mutineers.
> This warning is final.
> Trotsky, …﻿ ﻿. Chairman Revolutionary Military Soviet of the Republic.
> Kamenev, Commander-in-Chief.

===Chinese===

“工农政府严正下令：喀琅施塔得要塞以及所有抗命叛乱舰只，必须立即无条件绝对服从苏维埃共和国的最高权威！因此，我以全军最高名义命令所有胆敢向社会主义祖国举起反叛凶刃者，立刻就地放下武器投降！凡执迷不悟顽抗到底者，一律当场缴械并全员移送苏维埃革命法庭受审！被非法扣押的特派政委以及其他苏维埃政府代表，必须毫发无损立即就地获释！唯有无条件缴械投降者，方可宽大争取苏维埃共和国的从宽赦免！
与此同时，我已正式签发全军作战动员令，调集全军武装强力坚决铁血平定暴乱、以枪炮武装彻底彻底粉碎叛乱分子！由此给当地无辜和平居民所造成的一切附带流血伤亡灾难，其全部历史罪责一律全额由反革命暴乱匪首自行承担！
此令即为最后通牒，绝无宽宥余地！
共和国革命军事委员会主席：托洛茨基
全军武装部队总司令：加米涅夫（Kamenev）”

===Original===

The city is on the verge of panic. The factories are closed, and there are rumors of demonstrations and riots. Threats against Jews are becoming audible. Military forces continue to flow into Petrograd and environs. Trotsky has sent another demand to Kronstadt to surrender, the order containing the threat: “I’ll shoot you like pheasants.” Even some Communists are indignant at the tone assumed by the Government. It is a fatal error, they say, to interpret the workers’ plea for bread as opposition. Kronstadt’s sympathy with the strikers and their demand for honest elections have been turned by Zinoviev into a counterrevolutionary plot. I have talked the situation over with several friends, among them a number of Communists. We feel there is yet time to save the situation. A commission in which the sailors and workers would have confidence, could allay the roused passions and find a satisfactory solution of the pressing problems. It is incredible that a comparatively unimportant incident, as the original strike in the Trubotchny mill, should be deliberately provoked into civil war with all the bloodshed it entails.
The Communists with whom I have discussed the suggestion all favor it, but dare not take the initiative. No one believes in the Kozlovsky story. All agree that the sailors are the staunchest supporters of the Soviets; their object is to compel the authorities to grant needed reforms. To a certain degree they have already succeeded. The *zagraditelniye otryadi*, notoriously brutal and arbitrary, have been abolished in the Petrograd province, and certain labor organizations have been given permission to send representatives to the villages for the purchase of food. During the last two days special rations and clothing have also been issued to several factories. The Government fears a general uprising. Petrograd is now in an “extraordinary state of siege”; being out of doors is permitted only till nine in the evening. But the city is quiet. I expect no serious upheaval if the authorities can be prevailed upon to take a more reasonable and just course. In the hope of opening the road to a peaceful solution, I have submitted to Zinoviev a plan of arbitration signed by persons friendly to the Bolsheviki:

> To the Petrograd Soviet of Labor and Defense,

===Chinese===

整座城市濒临全面恐慌大崩溃的绝境边缘。各大工厂全线停产大门紧闭，关于街头即将爆发武装示威游行与流血暴动的大量传闻在民间四处疯传。街头巷尾甚至开始清晰可闻针对犹太人的刻毒反动咒骂。大批全副武装的正规野战重兵与精锐装甲部队，正源源不断向彼得格勒市区及其周边近郊滚滚集结开拔。托洛茨基向喀琅施塔得再次签发了一道勒令投降的严厉通牒，公文中赫然带着令人毛骨悚然的嗜血恐吓：“我将像猎杀野鸡一样把你们一个个全部当场击毙！”甚至连党内部分共产党员也对政府高层摆出的这副灭绝人性的跋扈腔调深表万分震惊与由衷义愤。大家在私下直言不讳地指出，将广大饥饿工人们为求一口活命面包的朴素请愿诉求，恶意扭曲解读为对抗政权的敌对政治阴谋，是一个致命而不可饶恕的弥天大错！水兵们出于阶级情谊对罢工工人的深切同情声援、以及他们要求实行真正公正民主的苏维埃选举的正当诉求，被季诺维也夫一手处心积虑罗织扭曲成了所谓的反革命叛乱大案。我同多位同仁深入交换了意见，其中也包括数名极具良知的共产党老布尔什维克。我们深切感到，在血雨腥风降临之前的最后一刻，局势依然尚存一线和平挽救的宝贵契机。组建一个由深受广大水兵与产业工人们高度信任的联合调解仲裁委员会，完全能够平息抚慰被煽动起来的暴戾狂热，并为眼前燃眉迫切的经济政治危机寻获一个合情合理、各方满意的圆满解决方案。因为特鲁博奇内工厂最初区区一场微不足道的普通罢工小摩擦，竟然被人为处心积虑激化挑动演变成一场同胞自相残杀、血流成河的残酷全面内战，这在人类理性面前简直是匪夷所思、滑天下之大稽的弥天荒唐！
所有同我深入探讨过此项和平调解动议的共产党员无一例外在心底深表赞成，却没有任何一个人胆敢冒天下之大不韪率先打破沉默挑头提议。私底下根本没有一个人真会愚蠢到采信所谓科兹洛夫斯基将军叛变夺权的拙劣宣传谎言。大家心照不宣一致公认水兵们恰恰正是全俄真正苏维埃民主制度最坚贞不屈的忠诚捍卫者；他们誓死抗争的崇高初衷，无非是迫使官僚当局不得不推行顺应民心的民主经济改革罢了。在某种程度上，他们的英勇抗争事实上已经迫使当局作出了实质性让步：臭名昭著、专横残暴至极的武装阻击缉粮队（zagraditelniye otryadi）已在彼得格勒全省境内被正式下令就地解散取缔，数家重点工会组织也已破例获准派遣采购代表直接深入广大农村自由采买平价粮食。在过去的短短2天里，当局甚至破天荒向数家核心大厂火速特批加发了防寒过冬棉衣与特供口粮配给。政府在内心深处对爆发全国性全面人民大起义充满了骨子里的极度恐惧！彼得格勒如今已被正式置于“非常军事戒严状态”的严酷铁箍之下；全城居民夜间外出活动的时间被严厉限制在晚上9点之前。然而，全城街头却显得出奇的平静肃穆。我深信，只要能极力劝导当权者改弦易辙重返更加理智、宽容与公道的文明轨道，一场骇人听闻的流血浩劫完全可以化险为夷。为了在最后关头给和平解决争端铺平道路，我正式向季诺维也夫呈递呈交了一份由多位对布尔什维克政权持真诚友好立场的各界知名人士联合联名签署的和平仲裁斡旋请愿书：
> “致彼得格勒劳动与国防苏维埃：

===Original===

Chairman Zinoviev:
> To remain silent now is impossible, even criminal. Recent events impel us Anarchists to speak out and to declare our attitude in the present situation.
> The spirit of ferment manifest among the workers and sailors is the result of causes that demand our serious attention. Cold and hunger had produced discontent, and the absence of any opportunity for discussion and criticism is forcing the workers and sailors to air their grievances in the open.
> White-guardist bands wish and may try to exploit this dissatisfaction in their own class interests. Hiding behind the workers and sailors they throw out slogans of the Constituent Assembly, of free trade, and similar demands.
> We Anarchists have long exposed the fiction of these slogans, and we declare to the whole world that we will fight with arms against any counterrevolutionary attempt, in cooperation with all friends of the Social Revolution and hand in hand with the Bolsheviki.
> Concerning the conflict between the Soviet Government and the workers and sailors, we hold that it must be settled not by force of arms, but by means of comradely agreement. Resorting to bloodshed, on the part of the Soviet Government, will not﻿—in the given situation﻿—intimidate or quieten the workers. On the contrary, it will serve only to aggravate matters and will strengthen the hands of the Entente and of internal counterrevolution.
> More important still, the use of force by the Workers’ and Peasants’ Government against workers and sailors will have a demoralizing effect upon the international revolutionary movement and will result in incalculable harm to the Social Revolution.
> Comrades Bolsheviki, bethink yourselves before it is too late! Do not play with fire: you are about to take a most serious and decisive step.
> We hereby submit to you the following proposition: Let a Commission be selected to consist of five persons, inclusive of two Anarchists. The Commission is to go to Kronstadt to settle the dispute by peaceful means. In the given situation this is the most radical method. It will be of international revolutionary significance.
> Alexander Berkman
> Emma Goldman
> Perkus
> Petrovsky
> Petrograd, March 5, 1921.

*March 6.*﻿—Today Kronstadt sent out by radio, A statement of its position. It reads:

===Chinese===

主席季诺维也夫同志：
> 面对眼前的严峻危局，在此刻继续保持沉默已绝无可能，甚至无异于严重的犯罪。连日来在彼得格勒爆发的惊天工潮风暴，迫使我们这些无政府主义者必须挺身而出，在当下的历史关头公开表明我们的严正立场与态度。
> 在广大产业工人与海军水兵中间所普遍弥漫滋生的强烈动荡怨愤情绪，其背后有着极其深刻厚重的现实社会根源，理应引起我们全体革命者的高度严肃正视与深切反思。饥寒交迫的严酷生存现实直接酿成了普遍的阶级不满，而任何正常思想交流、民主讨论与建设性批评监督渠道的全面被扼杀剥夺，更是逼迫广大工人和水兵不得不走上街头，在光天化日之下公开发出自己的悲愤呼喊。
> 各路白卫军反动余孽分子极有可能企图浑水摸鱼，为了其本阶级的反动私利大肆利用挑动这份普遍的不满民愤。他们躲藏在工人和水兵身后，暗中兜售立宪会议、恢复资本主义自由贸易以及类似反动口号的主张。
> 我们无政府主义者早在很久以前便彻底揭露了这些反动口号的虚伪本质，我们在此向全世界庄严宣誓：我们将同社会革命的一切真诚朋友并肩携手，同布尔什维克同志手挽着手，用手中的枪炮武器誓死击溃一切企图复辟的反革命反动图谋！
> 然而，针对苏维埃政府同广大工人与水兵之间爆发的这场尖锐矛盾冲突，我们坚决主张：必须通过无产阶级同志之间开诚布公的同志式友好协商来妥善化解，而决不能诉诸武装枪炮的同胞相残！在目前的极度紧绷危局下，苏维埃政府若悍然诉诸血腥镇压，绝不可能恐吓吓倒工人们，也绝不可能换来哪怕片刻的稳定宁静。相反，这只会让局势进一步激化滑向无法收拾的深渊，反而在事实上大大递刀子壮大了协约国帝国主义武装干涉军以及国内反动潜伏势力的反动力量！
> 尤为严峻致命的是，工农政府一旦对自己的工人兄弟与英雄水兵大开杀戒大动干戈，必将对全世界国际共产主义革命运动造成无可挽回的思想道德毁灭性打击，必将给伟大的社会革命事业带来无法估量的万劫不复的滔天巨创！
> 布尔什维克同志们，在历史的悲剧彻底铸成之前，请摸摸良心悬崖勒马吧！切莫玩火自焚：你们此刻正站在踏出最严峻、最致命决绝历史错误的历史悬崖边缘！
> 我们特此郑重向你们呈递如下和平斡旋解决方案：由各方协商联合推选组建一个由5人组成的联合和平调查调解仲裁委员会，其中包括2名无政府主义者代表。该委员会将立刻全权奔赴喀琅施塔得，通过同志式的和平协商政治手段妥善化解这场兄弟纷争。在当前的严峻危局下，这是最彻底、最切中时弊的根本解决良方。这一伟大的和平举措，必将具有不可磨灭的光辉国际革命历史意义！
> 亚历山大·伯克曼（Alexander Berkman）
> 爱玛·戈尔德曼（Emma Goldman）
> 佩尔库斯
> 彼得罗夫斯基
> 彼得格勒，1921年3月5日。”
3月6日。——今天喀琅施塔得军港通过无线电广播电台向全世界播发了一份庄严阐明其政治立场的公开宣言。宣言掷地有声地宣告：

===Original===

> Our cause is just, we stand for the power of Soviets, not parties. We stand for freely elected representatives of the laboring masses. The substitute Soviets manipulated by the Communist Party have always been deaf to our needs and demands; the only reply we have ever received was shooting.﻿ ﻿… Comrades! They deliberately pervert the truth and resort to most despicable defamation.﻿ ﻿… In Kronstadt the whole power is exclusively in the hands of the revolutionary sailors, soldiers and workers﻿—not with counterrevolutionists led by some Kozlovsky, as the lying Moscow radio tries to make you believe.﻿ ﻿… Do not delay, Comrades! Join us, get in touch with us: demand admission to Kronstadt for your delegates. Only they will tell you the whole truth and will expose the fiendish calumny about Finnish bread and Entente offers.
> Long live the revolutionary proletariat and the peasantry!
> Long live the power of freely elected Soviets.

*March 7.*﻿—Distant rumbling reaches my ears as I cross the Nevsky. It sounds again, stronger and nearer, as if rolling toward me. All at once I realize that artillery is being fired. It is 6 p.m. Kronstadt has been attacked!
Days of anguish and cannonading. My heart is numb with despair; something has died within me. The people on the streets look bowed with grief, bewildered. No one trusts himself to speak. The thunder of heavy guns rends the air.
*March 17.*﻿—Kronstadt has fallen today.
Thousands of sailors and workers lie dead in its streets. Summary execution of prisoners and hostages continues.
*March 18.*﻿—The victors are celebrating the anniversary of the Commune of 1871. Trotsky and Zinoviev denounce Thiers and Gallifet for the slaughter of the Paris rebels.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

“我们的正义事业光明磊落，我们誓死捍卫的是全体苏维埃的民主政权，而不是任何特定党派的一党独裁私利！我们誓死捍卫的是由广大劳苦大众通过真正自由公正民主选举产生的人民代表的崇高权力！由共产党暗箱操弄、欺世盗名的所谓伪苏维埃，对人民的苦难疾苦与正当诉求历来充耳不闻麻木不仁；我们以往所换来的唯一官僚答复，唯有一阵阵无情的枪炮镇压！……同志们！他们正在处心积虑颠倒黑白混淆视听，正在诉诸最下流无耻的人身污蔑与恶毒造谣中伤！……在喀琅施塔得，全部国家政权早已神圣而纯粹地牢牢掌握在革命水兵、红军战士与产业工人们自己的双手之中——这里根本不存在任何由所谓科兹洛夫斯基匪徒所操弄指挥的反革命暴乱分子，正如莫斯科官僚广播电台企图向你们灌输欺骗的弥天谎言那样！……同志们，行动起来莫要迟疑！加入我们的正义阵营吧，立刻同我们建立革命联络：向当局坚决要求准许你们的工人代表团亲自登岛踏入喀琅施塔得实地见证！唯有你们自己的亲密战友，才能当面向你们讲述全部的血泪真相，才能彻底揭穿关于所谓芬兰面包资助与协约国帝国主义金援的灭绝人性的恶毒栽赃诽谤！
革命无产阶级与广大劳动农民阶级万岁！
通过自由公正民主选举产生的真正苏维埃政权万岁！”
3月7日。——当我穿过涅瓦大街时，一阵阵遥远而沉闷的巨大轰鸣声隐隐撞击着我的耳膜。轰鸣声再度骤然响起，愈发震耳欲聋、愈发由远及近，仿佛正排山倒海般向我脚下的大地滚滚碾压而来。刹那间我如遭雷击恍然大悟：那是重炮开火的炮火轰鸣！时针正指向下午6点整。喀琅施塔得要塞遭到了武装强攻突袭！
那是度日如年、肝肠寸断的炮火连天血腥风暴岁月。我的心在一阵阵剧烈的绝望抽搐中彻底麻木死寂；我内心中某种最珍贵、最崇高的神圣信仰，在这一刻彻底死去了。大街上行色匆匆的百姓们满脸写满了悲苦与迷茫，个个神情恍惚低头疾走。全城鸦雀无声，没有一个人胆敢开口吐露半个字。唯有重型要塞攻城巨炮那撕心裂肺的凄厉咆哮声，在大气层中疯狂撕扯着彼得格勒的苍穹天空。
3月17日。——就在今天，喀琅施塔得要塞最终陷落了。
成千上万具英雄水兵与工人们的遗体，横七竖八凄惨陈尸在要塞冰冷的街头泥泞与冰面上。针对大批被俘战俘以及无辜扣押人质的大规模秘密就地枪决清算屠杀，正在血腥昼夜上演。
3月18日。——踩着累累白骨的胜利者们，正在全城大张旗鼓地隆重庆祝1871年巴黎公社建立五十周年的辉煌纪念日。托洛茨基与季诺维也夫在讲台上口若悬河，声嘶力竭地痛斥控诉当年梯也尔与加利费血腥屠杀巴黎起义公社社员的滔天暴行！……

## XXXIX Last Links in the Chain / XXXIX 锁链的最后几环
===Original===

Pensively Pushkin stands on his stone pedestal, viewing life flowing by on the square bearing his name. On the boulevard the trees are smiling with budding green, and promenaders bask in the April sun. Familiar sight of Moscow streets, yet with a strange new atmosphere about the people. The vision of Kronstadt had flashed across the city; its dead embers lie ashen gray on the faces. I sense the disconsolate spirit in the procession of diverse type and attire﻿—workmen in torn footgear, rags wrapped about their legs; students in black shirts belted at the waist, the tails fluttering in the breeze; peasants in *lapti* of woven straw, soldiers in long gray coats, and dark-skinned sons of the Caucasus in brighter colors. Young women mingle with them, in short skirts and bare legs, some wearing men’s boots. Most of them are painted, even the little girls. Boldly they gaze at the men, inviting them with their eyes.
Gay music sounds from the garden nearby. At the little tables white-aproned waiters serve food and drinks to the guests. Groups gather at the gate sullenly watching the novel scene. “*Bourzhooi!* Damned speculators!” they mutter. The NEP53 is at work.
All along the street stores have been opened, their windows washed, freshly painted signs announcing private ownership. Provisions in large quantity and variety are exposed to view. Resentfully men and women crowd on the sidewalk, their eyes devouring the tempting display. “No food for rations!” someone comments sarcastically. “That’s what we’ve been shedding our blood for!” a soldier exclaims with an oath.
On the corner a feminine voice hails me, “Ah, the American *tovarish*!” It is Lena, my young acquaintance of the raid of the Okhotny market, over a year ago. She looks very fragile, her paleness accentuated by her crimsoned lips. There is unwonted self-consciousness in her manner, and the pink mounts her face under my gaze. “You see, I didn’t manage to get away,” she says wearily.
“Get away?” I asked in surprise.
“Don’t you remember? It was America or﻿—,” she breaks off with a forced smile.
We are in front of a sumptuous delicatessen store. Men in starched shirts and white collars, looking offensively opulent, and elegantly dressed women carry their purchases with free, assured manner. Ragged children besiege them for alms. The passersby scowl at them angrily.

===Chinese===

普希金若有所思地伫立在坚硬的石质基座之上，俯瞰着以其光辉大名命名的广场上川流不息的人间百态。林荫大道两旁的林木抽出了嫩绿的新芽，仿佛在春风中舒展微笑，漫步的行人们正沐浴在4月的和煦艳阳之下。这是莫斯科（Moscow）街头司空见惯的熟悉街景，然而在往来行人的眉宇间，却流露出一股光怪陆离的异样全新氛围。喀琅施塔得（Kronstadt）要塞那场惨绝人寰的风暴血火，如同一道刺目的闪电划破了整座城市的夜空；要塞死难英烈那早已熄灭冷却的死灰余烬，仿佛化作了一层铅灰色的惨白死相，沉重烙印在每一个行人的脸庞之上。在这支由形形色色不同阶层、不同服饰拼凑而成的人流队列中，我深切呼吸到了那股痛彻心扉的凄凉与绝望：工人们脚蹬破烂不堪的破鞋，双腿上紧紧缠绕着发黄破烂的碎布条；青年大学生们身穿腰间紧束皮带的黑色俄式衬衫，宽大的衬衫下摆在春风中猎猎翻飞；老农们脚踩着稻草编织的树皮鞋（lapti），士兵们身披灰头土脸的粗呢长大衣，而来自高加索山区面色黝黑的少数民族同胞则身着色彩鲜艳亮丽的传统民族盛装。年轻的妇女们三三两两穿插其间，穿着超短的裙裾，露出光秃秃的大腿，脚上甚至穿着笨重的男式大皮靴。绝大多数女子脸上浓妆艳抹涂脂抹粉，甚至连未成年的垂髫幼女也不例外。她们目光大胆泼辣直勾勾地盯着过往的男人们，用挑逗放荡的眼神公然招揽着生意。
欢快悦耳的轻音乐从近旁的街心花园里悠扬飘出。在花园里的一张张白色小餐桌旁，系着雪白围裙的侍者们正毕恭毕敬地为宾客们端茶倒水、奉上精美的西餐美酒。一群群衣衫褴褛的平民百姓聚拢在大铁门外，满面阴云怒容满面地冷眼旁观着这幕光怪陆离的破天荒新奇图景。“资产阶级（Bourzhooi）！该千刀万剐的投机倒把黑商贩！”人群中咬牙切齿地低声咒骂道。新经济政策53（NEP）正在大行其道！
整条大街两旁的商铺店铺纷纷开门营业，玻璃橱窗被擦拭得一尘不染明净照人，粉刷一新的各式招牌赫然宣告着私人所有制的高调回归。堆积如山、琳琅满目的各式精美食品副食品被大张旗鼓地陈列在临街橱窗内公开展示。人行道上挤满了义愤填膺的男女百姓，一双双饥肠辘辘的眼睛贪婪饥渴地死死盯着橱窗内诱人的陈列。“给老百姓发口粮时永远叫嚷粮食告罄！”有人语带尖刻地冷嘲热讽道。“难道当年弟兄们抛头颅洒热血，换来的就是这帮畜生重新花天酒地吗？！”一名红军战士手指关节捏得咯咯作响破口大骂道。
在街角处，一个清脆的女声突然在背后惊喜呼唤我道：“啊，美利坚同志！”此人正是丽娜（Lena）——一年多前在奥霍特内猎人商行黑市突击搜捕中同我结识的那位年轻姑娘。她看起来纤弱单薄弱不禁风，两片涂抹得血红猩红的嘴唇更加反衬出面庞那病态的惨白。她的举止言谈间流露出一种异乎寻常的自惭形秽与神经质局促，在我的注视下，一抹羞赧的红晕悄然爬上了她的两腮。“你看，我终究还是没能逃出去，”她满脸倦容凄凉地说道。
“逃出去？”我惊诧地反问道。
“难道你忘了吗？要么逃去美洲，要么就只能沦为——”她嘴角泛起一丝惨淡苦涩的勉强微笑，欲言又止。
我们此刻正并肩伫立在一家陈设奢华的高级熟食商行大门前。身穿浆洗得笔挺硬领白衬衫、打着雪白领带的男人们，举手投足间散发着令人作呕的暴发户傲慢富态，而身着华丽考究晚礼服的贵妇名媛们，则迈着从容不迫、志得意满的高傲步伐拎着大包小包的名贵战利品扬长而去。一大群衣衫褴褛、面黄肌瘦的小叫花子乞丐一拥而上苦苦乞讨施舍。过往的普通百姓无不向这群新贵投去怒火中烧的刻毒白眼。

===Original===

“How many times I was arrested for ‘speculation,’ ” Lena remarks bitterly.
Remembering my visit to her home, I inquire after her family. “Mother, Baby, and Yasha died from typhus,” she replies dully. “That’s what the certificate said, but I know it was starvation.”
“Your cousin?”
“Oh, she is doing well. With some Communist. I’m all alone in the world now.”
“Poor Lena,” escapes me.
“Oh, I don’t want your pity,” she cries disconsolately. “Wish I’d died with mother.”
---
Further on the Tverskaya I find “Golos Truda,” the Anarchist publishing house, closed, a Cheka seal on the lock. A man is peering through the window at the havoc wrought within by the raiders. His Red Army cap does not conceal the fresh scars on his head. With surprise I recognize Stepan, my Petrograd soldier friend. He had been wounded in the Kronstadt campaign, he informs me; the Petrograd hospitals were crowded, and he was sent to Moscow. Now he has been discharged, but he is so weak he is barely able to walk.
“We crossed the Neva at night,” he relates; “all in white shrouds like so many ghosts﻿—you couldn’t tell us from the snow on the frozen river. Some of the boys didn’t want to advance,” he looks at me significantly. “The Communist detachment back of us trained machine guns on them﻿—there was no hesitating. The artillery belched from our side; some shots fell short, striking the ice just in front of us. In a flash whole companies disappeared, guns and all, sunk into the deep. It was a frightful night.” He pauses a moment; then, bending close to me, he whispers: “In Kronstadt I learned the truth. It’s we who were the counterrevolutionists.”
The Universalist Club on the Tverskaya is deserted, its active members imprisoned since the Kronstadt events. Anarchists from various parts have been brought to the city, and are now in the Butirki and Taganka jails. In connection with the growing labor discontent severe reprisals are taking place against the revolutionary element and the Communist Labor Opposition which demands industrial democracy.
The situation handicaps the work of the Peter Kropotkin Memorial Committee, in the interests of which I have come to the capital. The Moscow Soviet passed a resolution to aid “Golos Truda” in the publication of the complete works of the great Anarchist thinker, but the Government closed the establishment.

===Chinese===

“当年为了倒卖维生，我挨过契卡多少回抓捕入狱啊，”丽娜咬牙切齿辛酸地说道。
回想起当年登门造访她家的情景，我关切地打听起她家人的近况。“母亲、小毛头，还有亚沙（Yasha），全染上斑疹伤寒病死了，”她眼神空洞麻木地低声喃喃道。“医院开具的死亡证明上确实是这么写的，但我心里明镜似的，他们全是被活活饿死的。”
“你的表姐呢？”
“哼，人家如今混得风生水起滋润得很呢。傍上了一位有权有势的共产党大官。如今这茫茫天地间，就只剩下我一个人孤苦伶仃苟活在世上了。”
“可怜的丽娜，”我不禁发自肺腑地由衷哀叹道。
“收起你的假慈悲吧，我用不着任何人的同情可怜！”她痛不欲生近乎歇斯底里地哭喊道，“我真巴不得当初跟母亲一块死了倒干净！”
---
漫步在特维尔大街（Tverskaya）上，我赫然发现无政府主义出版重镇《劳动之声》（Golos Truda）的大门紧闭深锁，沉重的大锁上赫然贴着契卡（the Cheka）醒目刺眼的查封封条。一名头戴红军军帽的男子正将脸贴在冰冷的玻璃窗上，心惊肉跳地窥视着屋内被查抄洗劫抄家后的满地狼藉瓦砾。他头顶上的军帽遮掩不住头侧那道刚刚缝合不久的狰狞新刀伤痕迹。令我惊喜交加的是，我一眼认出了此人正是我在彼得格勒的红军战士老友斯捷潘（Stepan）。他神情黯然地告知我，自己在攻打喀琅施塔得的强攻战役中身负重伤；彼得格勒各大后方野战医院人满为患床位告罄，他被紧急转运后送到了莫斯科抢救。如今他刚刚办理了出院手续，但体质极度虚弱，甚至连双脚走路都在摇摇晃晃打晃。
“那天夜里我们踏冰强渡涅瓦河，”他压低嗓门向我娓娓道来；“全体突击队员全身上下披着雪白的伪装裹尸布，活脱脱像是一群来自地狱的幽灵冤魂——在冰封江面的皑皑白雪掩映下，根本辨认不出肉体和冰雪的区别。我们连队里好几个小伙子不忍心向自己的同胞骨肉兄弟开枪，不肯往前冲，”说到这里，他意味深长地凝视着我的眼睛。“尾随督战在我们身后的共产党特别督战队，立刻把重机枪枪口直直对准了他们的后脑勺——没有任何犹豫后退的余地。我们阵地后方的重炮群开始疯狂怒吼；有几发炮弹落点太近打偏了，不偏不倚正好砸在紧贴我们脚跟前方的坚冰上轰然炸开。转瞬之间，整连整排的鲜活战士连人带枪眨眼间人间蒸发，被无情卷入吞噬进了深不见底的冰冷江底旋涡。那真是一个毛骨悚然的人间地狱长夜啊！”他深深停顿了片刻；随即，整个人猛地凑近我的耳畔，用极其微弱却颤抖的嗓音贴耳耳语道：“直到攻进喀琅施塔得城里，我才终于彻底明白了血淋淋的历史真相。原来我们自己，才是那帮该千刀万剐的反革命刽子手啊！”
特维尔大街上的普世主义者俱乐部如今早已门庭冷落人去楼空，其核心骨干骨干自喀琅施塔得惨案爆发以来已被全数逮捕投入大牢。来自全国各个角落省份的无政府主义异见人士被秘密集中押解押送抵京，如今统统被羁押在布蒂尔卡监狱与塔甘卡（Taganka）大牢之中。伴随着产业劳工大众普遍动荡怨愤情绪的日趋白热化，当局针对革命左翼阵营以及公开要求实现工业民主管理的共产党工人反对派展开了雷霆万钧的铁血血腥大清洗大镇压。
急转直下的政治恐怖严冬，直接扼杀瘫痪了彼得·克鲁泡特金纪念筹备委员会的一切正常科学筹备工作，而我此番专程奔赴首都莫斯科的核心使命恰恰正是为了推进这项神圣工作。莫斯科苏维埃此前虽曾白纸黑字正式签署表决决议，大力资助扶持《劳动之声》出版社全额出版发行这位伟大无政府主义思想泰斗的全部生前著作文集，但官僚当局如今却在背地里千方百计下绊子百般阻挠拖延。

===Original===

The Soviet also donated the house where Kropotkin was born as a home for the Museum, but every attempt to get the place vacated by the Communist organization now occuping it has failed. The official attitude negates all our efforts.
*April 15.*﻿—Unexpected visitors today. I sat in my room (in the apartment of a private family in Leontievsky Alley) when an official entered, accompanied by the house porter and two soldiers. He introduced himself as an agent of the newly organized department “for the improvement of the workers’ mode of life,” and I could not suppress a smile when he solemnly informed me that the campaign for the benefit of the proletarians is directed by the Cheka. Better quarters are to be put at the disposal of the toilers, he announced; my room is among those to be “requisitioned” for that purpose. I should have to leave within twenty-four hours.
In entire sympathy with his object, I called the official’s attention to the utter impossibility of securing even bed space at such short notice. Permission and “assignments” must first be procured from the Housing Bureau, a procedure which at best takes a week’s time; often it requires months. Without deigning a reply the Chekist stepped into the hallway; opening the first door at hand, he said curtly: “You can stay here in the meantime.”
A burst of soapy vapor swept against us. Through the clouds of steam I discerned a bedstead, a little table, and a woman bending over the washtub.
“*Tovarish*, here lives﻿—,” the house porter remarked timidly.
“It’s big enough for two,” the official retorted.
“But it’s occupied by a woman,” I protested.
“You’ll manage somehow,” he laughed coarsely.
---
Days spent at the offices of the Housing Bureau bring no results. But a week passes, then another, and no tenants appear to claim my room. The department for the “improvement of the workers’ mode of life” is apparently more interested in “requisitioning” occupied lodgings than in putting them at the disposal of the proletarians. Only influence in high places or a generous “gift” secures the favor.54
Unexpectedly a friendly Communist comes to the rescue. It is arranged that my room be assigned to the Kropotkin Memorial Commission for an office: being the secretary I am permitted to retain it as my living quarters.
*April 30.*﻿—Dark rumors circulate in the city.

===Chinese===

莫斯科苏维埃虽曾象征性签署行政划拨批文，将克鲁泡特金当年呱呱坠地的故居老宅拨付给筹备委员会充当纪念博物馆馆舍，然而面对如今赖在老宅里拒不搬迁腾房的共产党机关单位，我们的一切清场交涉均如蚍蜉撼树碰壁告吹。官方骨子里的官僚敌意全面扼杀葬送了我们的一切民间努力。
4月15日。——今天寓所迎来了几位不速之客。当时我正坐在自己的书房里闭目沉思（我借住在列昂季耶夫小巷一户平民百姓的私人寓所内），房门突然被猛地推开，一名身穿制服的苏维埃官员在公寓门卫以及两名全副武装的士兵护卫下大摇大摆闯了进来。此人神气活现自我介绍是刚刚挂牌成立的所谓“改善工人生活方式专门委员会”的执行特派专员，当他一本正经地向我郑重宣称这项声称为了劳苦无产阶级切身福祉的浩大工程是由契卡（the Cheka）全权牵头指挥督办时，我嘴角忍不住掠过了一丝荒诞不经的冷笑。他趾高气昂地当场宣布，国家必须将更为宽敞体面的优渥住房腾挪出来供广大产业工人享用；而我的这间书房恰恰正是被列入强制“查抄征发”黑名单上的重点目标之一。他强硬勒令我必须在24小时内立刻全员卷铺盖滚蛋净身出户。
在从思想原则上对其声称的崇高政治口号深表充分理解同情的前提下，我平静地向这位官老爷据理力争指明：在如此仓促苛刻的通牒时限内，要在全城找到哪怕一张能容身的床位也是天方夜谭白日做梦。按苏维埃律令程序，必须先向住房局呈递申请公文并排队申领正式的“房屋配给令”，这一繁琐冗长的官僚审批流程在顺风顺水的最理想情况下也至少需要耗费整整一周光阴，而在绝大多数时候往往需要拖沓拉锯数月之久。契卡特派专员甚至不屑于赏脸做出任何正面答复；他冷笑着一把拉开走廊过道的第一扇顺手房门，用居高临下发号施令的生硬腔调冷冰冰地喝令道：“从今往后你先凑合给我窝在这里！”
一股浓烈呛鼻的肥皂热气瞬间扑面席卷而来。透过弥漫翻滚的浓密热蒸汽，我依稀辨认出屋里摆着一张逼仄的窄铁床、一张摇摇欲坠的旧小木桌，以及一位正吃力弯腰趴在巨大大洗衣木盆上洗衣服的中年苦难妇人。
“报告同志，这里住着的是——”门卫见状吓得战战兢兢，忍不住在一旁怯生生小声提醒道。
“这屋子塞下两个人绰绰有余！”特派专员蛮横跋扈地厉声打断顶了回去。
“可屋里明明住着一位单身女同志啊，这男女有别成何体统！”我义愤填膺当场抗议道。
“管他娘的什么男女有别，你们俩横竖自个儿想办法凑合着挤挤就是了！”他发出一阵令人作呕的下流狂妄狂笑声。
---
在住房局各大衙门里耗费了数天光阴四处奔波，一切努力均如泥牛入海毫无结果。然而一周光阴过去了，紧接着又是一周匆匆逝去，当初那位官老爷信誓旦旦声称即将进驻占领我书房的工人同志却连个影子都未曾出现。这个所谓的“改善工人生活方式专门委员会”，其真正的黑幕兴趣显然完全集中在如何以强权手段大肆“查抄腾空”现有人口居住的体面寓所本身，而压根毫无真正将其无私交由底层无产阶级居住享用的半点诚意。唯有深厚的通天官僚裙带后台、或是私下奉上一份沉甸甸的黄金美钞巨额“厚礼”，才能在这场肮脏的权力寻租游戏中博得高官的特权偏袒54。
所幸天无绝人之路，一位私交甚笃、深怀良知的共产党老布尔什维克关键时刻挺身而出仗义相助。经其巧妙周旋斡旋，当局正式签发红头批文，将我的这间书房正式破例划拨给克鲁泡特金纪念筹备委员会充当官方常设公办室：作为该委员会全权秘书长，我终于得以名正言顺获准继续留任居住在自己的这处寓所之内。
4月30日。——一连串令人不寒而栗的恐怖阴森传闻在全城地下暗流中疯狂疯传。

===Original===

Three hundred politicals are said to have disappeared from the Butirki prison. Removed by force at night, it is reported; some executed. The Cheka refuses information.
Several days pass in tortuous uncertainty﻿—many of my friends are among the missing. People living in the neighborhood of the prison tell of frightful cries heard that night and sounds of desperate struggle. The authorities profess complete ignorance.
Gradually the facts begin to leak out. It has become known that the fifteen hundred non-politicals in the Butirki had declared a hunger strike in protest against the unhygienic conditions. The cells were overcrowded and unspeakably filthy, the doors locked even by day, the toilet buckets seldom removed, poisoning the air with fetid smells. The sanitary commission had warned the administration of the imminent danger of an epidemic, but its recommendations were ignored. Then the strike broke out. On the fourth day some of the prisoners became hysterical. Unearthly yells and the rattling of iron doors shook the prison for hours, the uproar rousing the neighborhood in alarm. The politicals did not participate in the demonstration. Segregated in a separate wing, they had by collective action compelled concessions. Their situation was much more tolerable than that of the “common” prisoners. But their sense of human kinship determined them to intercede. Their expostulations finally induced the Cheka to declare the demands of the hunger strikers just and to promise immediate relief. Thereupon the “commoners” terminated their protest, and the incident was apparently closed.
But a few days later, on the night of April 25, a detachment of soldiers and Chekists suddenly appeared in the prison. One by one the cells of the politicals were attacked, the men beaten and the women dragged by their hair into the yard, most of them in their night clothes. Some of the victims, fearing they were being taken to execution, resisted. Butts of guns and revolvers silenced them. Overcome, they were forced into automobiles and taken to the railroad station.
Investigation by the Moscow Soviet has now elicited the information that the kidnapped politicals, comprising Mensheviki, Social Revolutionists of the Right and Left, and Anarchists, have been isolated in rigorous solitary in the most dreaded Tsarist prisons in Ryazan, Orlov, Yaroslavl, and Vladimir.
*June.

===Chinese===

坊间言之凿凿盛传，多达300名政治犯在一夜之间从布蒂尔卡监狱中神秘人间蒸发。据可靠内幕披露，这批同志是在深更半夜遭到军警武装强行暴力押解押出牢房的；有人甚至已被秘密就地枪决正法。契卡对此实行了最严苛的新闻封锁，断然拒绝透露半点风声。
在度日如年的煎熬与焦灼折磨中，数天光阴匆匆流逝——我的多名至交战友挚友赫然列于失踪名单之上。常年居住在布蒂尔卡监狱高墙外周边的街坊邻里惊恐万状地向外界讲述，事发当夜大牢深处曾传出惊天动地的凄厉呼救哭号以及殊死搏斗扭打的骇人声响。然而苏维埃官僚当局却揣着明白装糊涂，在公开场合坚称对此一无所知。
随着时间的推移，血淋淋的历史真相终于开始从铁壁合围中一点一滴泄漏出来。原来，关押在布蒂尔卡监狱里的整整1500名刑事普通在押犯人，为了公开抗议狱方灭绝人性的非人道恶劣卫生环境，此前曾毅然发动了集体绝食大抗议。牢房里人满为患拥挤不堪，卫生状况肮脏恶臭令人作呕，甚至在青天白日之下牢房铁门也被死死反锁上栓，盛装排泄物的马桶便桶极少按时清理倾倒，致命的恶臭毒气充斥窒息着每一寸空气。官方防疫卫生委员会曾数度正式向监狱当局呈递书面警告公函，严正指出大牢内部爆发毁灭性急性传染大瘟疫的巨大凶险，然而监狱官僚对此却置若罔闻视若无睹。绝望之中，大罢工大绝食风暴终于彻底引爆了。绝食进行到第4天时，部分神经高度脆弱的囚犯精神防线全面崩溃失控。令人毛骨悚然的人间地狱般凄厉尖叫声、以及疯狂摇晃砸击铁门的金属撞击巨响整整震动撕裂了监狱大楼数小时之久，巨大的骚乱喧嚣声吓得四邻街坊魂飞魄散如临大敌。被单独集中关押在独立监区的政治犯同志们起初并未直接参与这场绝食示威。作为享有特殊政治待遇的独立群体，他们此前凭借坚贞不屈的集体斗争，早已迫使狱方做出了相当程度的人道妥协。他们的铁窗境遇远比那些被侮辱与被损害的“刑事普通犯”要优渥体面得多。然而，源自革命阶级骨肉同胞的崇高人道主义良知与阶级情谊，驱使他们毅然决然挺身而出为普通犯人呼吁请命。在政治犯集体义正词严的强力交涉斡旋下，契卡最终不得不公开低头妥协，公开承认绝食工农群众的正当诉求完全合情合理合法，并白纸黑字庄严立下字据承诺立即予以实质性整改落实。目睹官僚低头，普通犯人们这才宣告中止绝食抗争，整场惊天工潮风波表面上看似乎已圆满画上了句号。
然而仅仅过了几天，在4月25日那个漆黑如墨的恐怖深夜里，一队全副武装的野战士兵与契卡刽子手突然荷枪实弹如恶狼般扑进了大牢。他们挨个牢房疯狂暴力砸开政治犯的监舍大门，将男同志们打得皮开肉绽头破血流，将女同志们揪着头发从床上硬生生拖拽到冰冷泥泞的大院里，绝大多数女受害者甚至根本来不及穿上外衣、全身上下仅裹着薄薄的单薄睡衣。部分被捕同志误以为死期已到、以为自己即将被秘密押赴刑场执行枪决，便爆发出绝望的人性殊死反抗。然而在冰冷坚硬的枪托与左轮手枪枪柄的疯狂劈头盖脸暴打之下，反抗的呼喊被生生扼杀打断打碎。被打得奄奄一息失去意识的受害者们被像装死猪一样硬生生塞进重型军用卡车，风驰电掣押往火车站连夜分流转运。
经莫斯科苏维埃内部良知人士的深入秘密追查，如今确凿的事实证据终于大白于天下：这批在深夜被军警暴力绑架绑票的政治犯同志，涵盖了孟什维克、左翼与右翼社会革命党人、以及大批坚贞不屈的无政府主义革命志士，如今他们已被彻底分散打散，被秘密押往梁赞、奥廖尔、雅罗斯拉夫尔以及弗拉基米尔等沙皇旧俄时代最令人闻风丧胆的阴森死牢中，实行了最灭绝人性的残酷单独禁闭断联。
6月。

===Original===

*﻿—Intensive preparations are being made for the reception of the foreign delegations. The Congress of the Comintern (Communist International) and first Conference of the Red Trade Unions are to be held simultaneously.
The city is in holiday attire. Red flags and banners decorate official buildings and the residences of prominent Bolsheviki. The filth of months is carted off the streets; swarms of child hucksters are being arrested; the beggars have disappeared from their customary haunts, and the Tverskaya is cleared of prostitutes. The main thoroughfares are emblazoned with revolutionary mottoes, and colored posters illustrate the “triumph of Communism.”
In the Hotel Luxe, palatial hostelry of the capital, are quartered the influential representatives of the foreign Communist parties. The street in front is lined with automobiles; I recognize the Royce of Karakhan and Zinoviev’s machine from the garages of the Kremlin. Frequent tours are arranged to places of historic interest and Bolshevik meccas, always under the guidance of attendants and interpreters selected by the Cheka. Within there is an atmosphere of feverish activity. The brilliant banquet hall is crowded. The velvety cushions and bright foliage of the smoking room are restful to the delegates of the Western proletariat.
On the sidewalk opposite the Hotel women and children lurk in the hallways. Furtively they watch the soldiers unloading huge loaves of bread from a truck. A few chunks have fallen to the ground﻿—the urchins dart under the wagon in a mad scramble.
All traffic is suspended on the Theatre Square. Soldiers in new uniforms and polished boots, and mounted troopers form a double chain around the big square, completely shutting off access. Only holders of special cards, provided with photographs and properly attested, are permitted to pass to the Big Theatre. The Congress of the Comintern is in session.
*July 4.*﻿—Polyglot speech fills my room far into the small hours of the morning. Delegates from distant lands call to discuss Russia and the Revolution. As in a dream they vision the glory of revelation and are thrilled with admiration for the Bolsheviki. With glowing fervor they dwell on the wondrous achievements of Communism.

===Chinese===

全城正在紧锣密鼓地开展着迎接外国代表团的狂热筹备工作。共产国际（Comintern）全俄代表大会与红色工会国际（赤色职工国际）第一届世界代表大会即将在莫斯科联合联袂隆重召开。
整座城市披上了盛大的节日红装。鲜艳夺目的红旗、宽大宽宏的横幅标语精心装裱点缀着每一座苏维埃政府大厦以及布尔什维克实权显贵们的豪华私邸。街头巷尾堆积了整整好几个月的发臭陈年垃圾被连夜突击清运一空；成群结队在街头沿街乞讨倒卖火柴香烟的流浪孤儿儿童小商贩遭到了军警的大肆抓捕收押；乞丐盲流在一夜之间从他们惯常流落盘踞的街头彻底人间蒸发，而特维尔大街（Tverskaya）两旁的流莺暗娼更是被军警清洗扫荡得干干净净。各处核心干道两旁的建筑物外墙上，浓墨重彩地刷满了各式各样的革命战斗口号标语，一幅幅构思宏伟的大幅彩色宣传招贴画在大肆向全世界炫耀着“共产主义的辉煌彻底胜利”。
在首都最豪华尊贵、金碧辉煌的宫殿级国宾馆卢克斯酒店（Hotel Luxe）里，戒备森严地安顿下榻着来自世界各国共产党支部的举足轻重的重要外宾代表。酒店门前的大街上整整齐齐并排停放着一列列奢华名贵的名牌轿车；我一眼便认出了卡拉汉（Karakhan）那辆尊贵显赫的劳斯莱斯专车，以及季诺维也夫直接从克里姆林宫专用车库调拨调运而来的豪华座驾。当局精心组织安排了一趟趟走马灯般的红色红色历史圣地朝圣巡游，路线涵盖了各大著名革命纪念馆以及布尔什维克的权力麦加，然而每一次出行均毫无例外是在由契卡亲自挑选甄别政审合格的贴身陪同人员与专职翻译的严密陪同监视下开展的。酒店内部弥漫着一股热火朝天、令人窒息的繁忙外交景象。灯火辉煌的国宴大厅里人头攒动觥筹交错。吸烟室里高档柔软的天鹅绒沙发靠垫与郁郁葱葱的奇花异草，为这些远道而来的西方无产阶级尊贵代表提供了最极致惬意的身心休憩避风港。
而在酒店正对面的人行道上，一群群面黄肌瘦的苦难妇女与枯瘦如柴的孤儿饥民却正蜷缩在阴暗潮湿的门洞阴影里瑟瑟发抖。他们瞪大着一双双充满惊恐饥渴的眼睛，偷偷摸摸窥视着荷枪实弹的士兵正从一辆重型卡车上往下卸运一箩筐一箩筐香气扑鼻的巨大白面包。有几块拳头大小的烤面包在搬运中不慎跌落滚落到了泥泞的街面上——刹那间，几个饿得脱相的小叫花子发了疯一般从阴影中猛扑而出，像野狗夺食一般在卡车大轮子底下扭打厮打翻滚抢夺那几口活命的面包屑。
整个剧院广场（Theatre Square）如今已被全面戒严交通管制。身穿崭新笔挺呢子军服、脚蹬锃亮照人大皮靴的正规军卫队以及全副武装的英挺骑兵连，在宏伟宽阔的大广场外围筑起了两道密不透风的铁血人墙警戒线，彻底切断封锁了一切民间平民通行的可能。唯有持有盖有特批钢印公章、附带个人免冠近照并经政治保卫局严格政审备案的特种请柬代表，方被准许踏入大剧院（Big Theatre）的辉煌殿堂。共产国际的第三次世界代表大会此刻正在大厅内隆重开幕。
7月4日。——操着形形色色世界多国语言的异国喧哗高谈阔论，常常在我的小书房里彻夜回荡，一直持续到凌晨黎明破晓时分。来自天南海北遥远异邦的工运代表们纷纷登门造访，急切同我深入探讨俄罗斯苏俄革命的现实与前途。在他们眼中，这一切仿佛宛如身处一场波澜壮阔的崇高梦境之中，他们深信自己正在亲眼见证神圣历史天启降临的无上荣耀，对布尔什维克的丰功伟绩充满了由衷的顶礼膜拜与狂热敬仰。他们怀着炽热滚烫的宗教般虔诚，口若悬河地由衷赞美着共产主义在这片东方古老土地上创造的种种旷世人间奇迹。

===Original===

Like a jagged scalpel their naive faith tears at my heart where bleeding lie my own high hopes, the hopes of my first days in Russia, deflowered and blighted by the ruthless hand of dictatorship.
Most sanguine and confident are the latest arrivals, secluded in the atmosphere of the Luxe and entirely unfamiliar with the life and thought of the people. Fascinated and awed, they marvel at the genius of the Party and its amazing success. Tyranny and oppression in Russia are things of the past, they believe; the masses have become free, for the first time in the annals of man. Ignorance and poverty, the evil heritage of Tsardom and long civil war, will soon be outlived, and plenty shall be the birthright of everyone in the land where the disinherited have become the masters of life.
Occasionally in the discussion a discordant note is sounded by the new economic policy. The seeming deflection from avowed principles is perplexing. Does it not hold the menace of returning capitalism? A smile of benevolent superiority waves the timid questioner aside. The NEP is ingenious camouflage, he is assured. It is of no particular significance﻿—at most, it is a temporary expedient, an economic Brest-Litovsk in a way, to be swept away at the first blast of revolution in the West.
